        ) if E > 1 else torch.zeros(())
        probs = torch.softmax(logits, dim=1)
        if E > 1:
            r_ent = (-(probs * probs.clamp_min(1e-30).log()).sum(dim=1)
                     / torch.log(torch.tensor(float(E)))).mean()
        else:
            r_ent = torch.zeros(())
        return RoutingMetrics(
            usage_fraction=[float(v) for v in f],
            effective_experts=float(pr),
            load_max_over_mean=float(f.max() / f.mean().clamp_min(1e-30)),
            usage_entropy_normalised=float(ent),
            router_entropy_mean=float(r_ent),
            n_tokens=T,
        )

    def routing_metrics(self, x: Tensor) -> RoutingMetrics:
        x2 = x.reshape(-1, x.shape[-1])
        with torch.no_grad():
            logits, _, top_idx = self._route(x2)
            return self.routing_metrics_from(logits, top_idx)

    def all_active_forward(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
        """Partial sum over *every* expert -- must equal `dense_forward`."""
        y, _, _ = self._forward_impl(x, k=self.n_experts, combine="sum")
        return y

    def expert_outputs(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
        """Per-expert contributions, shape (n_experts, T, hidden).

        Materialising every expert is what the pilot called the *oracle* setting;
        exposing it here lets the PoC reuse `ffn.oracle_topk_residual` on the
        trained weights, so "trained" and "frozen" are measured the same way.
        """
        shape = x.shape
        x2 = x.reshape(-1, shape[-1])
        outs = []
        for e in range(self.n_experts):
            units = self.expert_units(e)
            he = F.silu(F.linear(x2, self.gate.index_select(0, units))) \
                * F.linear(x2, self.up.index_select(0, units))
            outs.append(F.linear(he, self.down.index_select(1, units)))
        return torch.stack(outs, dim=0)

    def router_scores(self, x: Tensor) -> Tensor:
        """Router logits as (T, n_experts), float32, no grad graph."""
        x2 = x.reshape(-1, x.shape[-1])
        with torch.no_grad():
            return self._router_logits(x2).detach()

    def dense_equivalent_error(self, x: Tensor) -> float:
        with torch.no_grad():
            ref = self.dense_forward(x)
            got = self.all_active_forward(x)
            return float((ref.detach() - got.detach()).norm()
                         / ref.detach().norm().clamp_min(1e-12))

    def describe(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "n_experts": self.n_experts,
            "top_k": self.top_k,
            "combine": self.config.combine,
            "plan": self.config.plan,
            "hidden": self.hidden_dim,
            "intermediate": self.intermediate,
            "expert_sizes": self.expert_sizes(),
            "router_params": int(sum(p.numel() for p in self.router.parameters())),
            "expert_params": int(self.gate.numel() + self.up.numel()
                                 + self.down.numel()),
        }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# model-level helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def upcycle_model(model: nn.Module, config: MoEConfig,
                  layers: list[int] | None = None,
                  mlp_attr: str = "mlp") -> list[tuple[int, MoEFFN]]:
    """Replace `model.model.layers[i].mlp` with a `MoEFFN` for each target layer.

    Returns [(layer_index, module), ...]. `layers=None` means every layer.
    """
    blocks = model.model.layers
    if layers is None:
        layers = list(range(len(blocks)))
    out: list[tuple[int, MoEFFN]] = []
    for i in layers:
        if not 0 <= i < len(blocks):
            raise ValueError(f"layer {i} out of range (0..{len(blocks) - 1})")
        block = blocks[i]
        moe = MoEFFN.from_hf_mlp(getattr(block, mlp_attr), config)
        setattr(block, mlp_attr, moe)
        out.append((i, moe))
    return out


def iter_moe_modules(model: nn.Module):
    for m in model.modules():
        if isinstance(m, MoEFFN):
            yield m


def collect_total_aux_loss(model: nn.Module) -> Tensor:
    """Sum of every MoE layer's weighted aux + z loss for the last forward."""
    total = None
    for m in iter_moe_modules(model):
        t = m.total_aux_loss()
        total = t if total is None else total + t
    if total is None:
        return torch.zeros((), device=next(model.parameters()).device)
    return total


def router_parameters(moes) -> list[Parameter]:  # noqa: F821
    params = []
    for _, m in moes:
        params += list(m.router.parameters())
    return params


def expert_parameters(moes) -> list[Parameter]:  # noqa: F821
    params = []
    for _, m in moes:
        params += [m.gate, m.up, m.down]
    return params


def set_top_k(moes, k: int) -> None:
    """Mutate the top-k of already-built modules (for the dense-limit check)."""
    for _, m in moes:
        m.config = m.config.with_(top_k=k)


def reconstruction_residual(reference: Tensor, got: Tensor) -> float:
    """Relative L2 error of `got` against `reference`.

    Same metric as `ffn.oracle_topk_residual`, so a trained MoE's residual can
    be compared directly against the frozen ceiling the pilot measured.
    """
    ref = reference.float()
    return float((ref - got.float()).norm() / ref.norm().clamp_min(1e-12))


===== experiments/branch_poc.py =====

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
branch_poc.py -- does *branching* into complete modules beat *slicing* them?

The question
------------
E14/E15 "upcycled" a dense FFN by **slicing** the intermediate axis into E
disjoint experts and keeping k. That loses most of the layer output, and the
loss only vanishes as k -> E. The standing objection is that real MoE models do
not do that: their experts are *complete* feed-forward networks, so activating
one performs a whole layer's maths and there is no partial-sum deficit.

This driver measures both constructions on the same model, the same activations
and the same metric, and separates two things that are easy to conflate:

    "slicing hurts"        (a claim about tiles)
    "reading less hurts"   (a claim about width)

The separation is done by comparing a **tile** against a **replica at the same
active width**. A replica has one distinct block repeated, so the router has
nothing to choose and its residual is the residual of that one block; a tile can
pick the *best* block, so the oracle does at least as well. If both improve with
width in the same way, the governing variable is width, not tiling -- which is
the whole argument, and it is measurable rather than assertable.

What is reported
----------------
All residuals are against the **original dense** output, which is the only
reference that means the same thing for a tile and a replica. (`oracle_topk` in
`moeme.ffn` compares against the sum of all experts, which is the dense layer
for a tile but E x the dense layer for a replica -- so it cannot be used here.)

* `residual_best_single_expert` -- best possible single expert, per token
* `residual_oracle_topk`       -- best possible top-k set, per token
* `residual_router`            -- what the router actually picks
* `residual_all_experts_sum`   -- faithfulness of keeping every expert

Usage
-----
    python3 experiments/branch_poc.py --layers 0 3 8 23 --steps 150 \
        --out results/branch_poc.json
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import importlib.util
import json
import os
import sys
import time

import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F
from torch import Tensor

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(HERE, "..", "src"))

from moeme.branch import BranchConfig, BranchMoEFFN          # noqa: E402


def _load_sibling(name: str):
    """Import `experiments/<name>.py` by path; `experiments/` is not a package."""
    path = os.path.join(HERE, f"{name}.py")
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(f"_poc_{name}", path)
    mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    sys.modules[spec.name] = mod
    spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
    return mod


POC = _load_sibling("upcycle_poc")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# domains: the data each *branch* is trained on independently (stage 2)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Branch-Train-MiX trains each branch on a different slice of the data before
# the branches are combined. Doing that on genuinely different *domains* is what
# gives the router something to learn: if every expert sees the same mixture,
# they converge to the same function and the router has nothing to choose. Each
# entry is (label, dataset, config or None, split, text field).
#
# Deliberately not `bigcode/the-stack-smol` for the code branch: it is gated, and
# a gated dataset silently costs you a branch instead of raising where it hurts.
DOMAINS = [
    ("code", "ise-uiuc/Magicoder-OSS-Instruct-75K", None, "train", "problem"),
    ("math", "meta-math/MetaMathQA-40K", None, "train", "query"),
    ("chat", "HuggingFaceH4/ultrachat_200k", None, "train_sft", "prompt"),
    ("wiki", "Salesforce/wikitext", "wikitext-103-raw-v1", "train", "text"),
]


def load_domain_text(label, dataset, config, split, field, want_chars) -> str:
    """Up to `want_chars` characters of one domain, or "" if it cannot load."""
    from datasets import load_dataset
    try:
        if config:
            ds = load_dataset(dataset, config, split=split, streaming=True)
        else:
            ds = load_dataset(dataset, split=split, streaming=True)
    except Exception as e:                                      # noqa: BLE001
        print(f"  domain {label}: {dataset} unavailable ({type(e).__name__})")
        return ""
    chunks, have = [], 0
    try:
        for row in ds:
            t = row.get(field)
            if not isinstance(t, str) or len(t) < 200:
                continue
            chunks.append(t)
            have += len(t)
            if have >= want_chars:
                break
    except Exception as e:                                      # noqa: BLE001
        print(f"  domain {label}: stream ended early ({type(e).__name__})")
    print(f"  domain {label}: {have:,} chars from {len(chunks)} docs")
    return "\n".join(chunks)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# the grid
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def config_table(inter: int) -> list[dict]:
    """The measurement grid.

    Two ladders plus the decisive comparison:

    * tile ladder   -- E in {8,4,2,1} at k=1 gives active widths 12.5/25/50/100%,
                       and reproduces the shape of the pilot's curve.
    * replica ladder -- the *same* widths built from a single repeated block, so
                       the router has no choice to make.
    * replica_full  -- complete modules, k=1: the "cut it into real sub-models"
                       proposal. Exact by construction, at E x the storage.
    """
    u8, u4, u2 = inter // 8, inter // 4, inter // 2
    return [
        # --- tile ladder (the E15 construction) ---------------------------
        dict(name="tile_E8_k1_12pct", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=8, top_k=1, init="tile", expert_units=u8)),
        dict(name="tile_E4_k1_25pct", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=1, init="tile", expert_units=u4)),
        dict(name="tile_E2_k1_50pct", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=2, top_k=1, init="tile", expert_units=u2)),
        dict(name="tile_E1_k1_100pct", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=1, top_k=1, init="tile", expert_units=inter)),
        dict(name="tile_E4_k2_50pct", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=2, init="tile", expert_units=u4)),
        dict(name="tile_E4_k4_100pct", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=4, init="tile", expert_units=u4)),
        # --- replica ladder: same widths, one repeated block --------------
        dict(name="replica_E4_k1_12pct", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=u8)),
        dict(name="replica_E4_k1_25pct", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=u4)),
        dict(name="replica_E4_k1_50pct", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=u2)),
        # --- the proposal: complete modules ------------------------------
        dict(name="replica_E4_k1_full", combine="sum", train=False,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=inter)),
        # --- trainee set: softmax, because `sum` gives the router no
        #     gradient from the output at all (see branch.py's docstring) ---
        dict(name="trained_tile_E4_k1_25pct", combine="softmax", train=True,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=1, init="tile", expert_units=u4)),
        dict(name="trained_replica_E4_k1_full", combine="softmax", train=True,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=inter)),
        dict(name="trained_replica_E4_k1_full_shared", combine="softmax",
             train=True,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=4, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=inter,
                      n_shared=1)),
    ]


def btx_table(inter: int, n_domains: int) -> list[dict]:
    """The branch-train-miX configs: complete modules, trained apart, then mixed.

    Two widths, because the whole argument of this study is about width:

    * `full`  -- every expert a complete FFN. `k=1` reads full width, so the
                 result should match the dense model. Cost: `E` x the storage.
    * `half`  -- each expert half width. `k=1` reads 50% of the layer, the same
                 active width as `tile_E4_k1_25pct`/`replica_E4_k1_50pct`, so
                 the three are directly comparable at equal active compute.

    Both train their experts independently on different domains first, then get
    the same short cooperation finetune. That is the comparison that says
    whether specialisation buys back what width costs.
    """
    return [
        dict(name=f"btx_replica_k1_full", combine="softmax", train=True, btx=True,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=n_domains, top_k=1, init="replica",
                      expert_units=inter)),
        dict(name=f"btx_replica_k1_half", combine="softmax", train=True, btx=True,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=n_domains, top_k=1, init="replica",
                      expert_units=inter // 2)),
        dict(name=f"btx_tile_k1_25pct", combine="softmax", train=True, btx=True,
             cfg=dict(n_experts=n_domains, top_k=1, init="tile",
                      expert_units=inter // n_domains)),
    ]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# model surgery
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def branchify(model, layers, cfg: BranchConfig):
    """Replace `layers[i].mlp` with a `BranchMoEFFN` built from it."""
    blocks = model.model.layers
    out = []
    for i in layers:
        moe = BranchMoEFFN.from_hf_mlp(getattr(blocks[i], "mlp"), cfg)
        setattr(blocks[i], "mlp", moe)
        out.append((i, moe))
    return out


def restore(blocks, originals) -> None:
    for i, m in originals:
        setattr(blocks[i], "mlp", m)


@torch.no_grad()
def residual_across_layers(moes, reference, device) -> dict:
    """Mean residual_report over the upcycled layers."""
    acc: dict[str, list[float]] = {}
    per_layer = {}
    for i, m in moes:
        ref = reference.get(i)
        if ref is None:
            continue
        rep = m.residual_report(ref["x"].to(device))
        per_layer[i] = rep
        for k, v in rep.items():
            acc.setdefault(k, []).append(float(v))
    mean = {k: sum(v) / len(v) for k, v in acc.items()}
    mean["per_layer"] = per_layer
    return mean


def accounting(moes) -> dict:
    """What the sparse layer costs to run versus to store."""
    return {
        "active_params": sum(m.active_params for _, m in moes),
        "stored_params": sum(m.stored_params for _, m in moes),
        "active_width_fraction": sum(m.active_width_fraction for _, m in moes)
                                 / max(len(moes), 1),
        "stored_width_fraction": sum(m.total_width_fraction for _, m in moes)
                                 / max(len(moes), 1),
        "n_layers": len(moes),
    }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# training
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def branch_train_experts(model, moes, args, domain_blocks, device, amp_dtype,
                         scaler) -> dict:
    """Stage 2 of branch-train-miX: train each expert alone on its own domain.

    Every expert in every upcycled layer is forced to serve the *whole* batch
    (router bypassed, `set_force_expert`) and trained only on its own domain's
    tokens. The experts never see each other here, so nothing makes them
    similar except their shared initialisation -- which is exactly the point:
    they start identical and are pushed apart by the data.

    No router is trained in this stage, because the router is not in the
    forward path and so receives no gradient. It is learned in stage 3.
    """
    E = moes[0][1].E
    if len(domain_blocks) != E:
        raise ValueError(f"btx needs one domain per expert: {len(domain_blocks)}"
                         f" shards for E={E}")
    per_expert, totals = [], {"steps": 0, "wall_seconds": 0.0}
    for e in range(E):
        blocks = domain_blocks[e]
        if not blocks:
            print(f"    expert {e}: no data, skipped")
            continue
        for _, m in moes:
            m.set_force_expert(e)
        print(f"    -- expert {e} alone on {len(blocks)} own-domain blocks --")
        st = train_model(model, moes, args, blocks, device, amp_dtype, scaler,
                         steps=args.branch_steps)
        per_expert.append({"expert": e, "blocks": len(blocks), **st})
        totals["steps"] += st["steps_completed"]
        totals["wall_seconds"] += st["wall_seconds"]
    for _, m in moes:
        m.set_force_expert(None)
    return {"per_expert": per_expert, **totals}


def train_model(model, moes, args, train_blocks, device, amp_dtype, scaler,
                steps: int | None = None):
    """Short continued-pretraining run. Only the MoE modules move."""
    steps = args.steps if steps is None else steps
    for p in model.parameters():
        p.requires_grad_(False)
    trainable = []
    for _, m in moes:
        for p in m.parameters():
            p.requires_grad_(True)
            trainable.append(p)

    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(trainable, lr=args.lr, weight_decay=args.weight_decay,
                            betas=(0.9, 0.95))
    gen = torch.Generator().manual_seed(args.seed)
    model.train()
    curve, t0 = [], time.perf_counter()
    accum = max(1, args.grad_accum)
    done = 0
    for step in range(steps):
        opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
        total = 0.0
        for _ in range(accum):
            j = int(torch.randint(len(train_blocks), (1,), generator=gen).item())
            x, y = train_blocks[j]
            x, y = x.to(device), y.to(device)
            if amp_dtype is not None:
                with torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=amp_dtype):
                    out = model(x, labels=y)
                    # `total_aux_loss` already applies the config's coefficient;
                    # scaling it again here would silently halve the effective
                    # weighting relative to the E14/E15 runs.
                    aux = sum(m.total_aux_loss() for _, m in moes)
                    loss = (out.loss + aux) / accum
                scaler.scale(loss).backward()
            else:
                out = model(x, labels=y)
                aux = sum(m.total_aux_loss() for _, m in moes)
                loss = (out.loss + aux) / accum
                loss.backward()
            total += float(loss.item())
        if scaler.is_enabled():
            scaler.unscale_(opt)
            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(trainable, args.clip)
            scaler.step(opt)
            scaler.update()
        else:
            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(trainable, args.clip)
            opt.step()
        done = step + 1
        every = max(1, min(steps // 5, 25))
        if step % every == 0 or step == steps - 1:
            el = time.perf_counter() - t0
            curve.append({"step": step, "loss": total, "elapsed": el})
            print(f"      step {step:4d}  loss {total:.4f}  elapsed {el:6.0f}s",
                  flush=True)
    model.eval()
    for p in model.parameters():
        p.requires_grad_(False)
    return {"steps_completed": done, "loss_curve": curve,
            "wall_seconds": time.perf_counter() - t0,
            "trainable_params": sum(p.numel() for p in trainable)}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# main
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# self-test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def self_test() -> int:
    """Offline check of the module and the driver's own plumbing.

    Runs in seconds on CPU with no model download, so a dtype or shape
    regression is found here rather than 20 minutes into a GPU run. Each
    assertion is an identity the construction is supposed to guarantee.
    """
    torch.manual_seed(0)
    H, I = 32, 64
    gate = torch.randn(I, H) * 0.5
    up = torch.randn(I, H) * 0.5
    down = torch.randn(H, I) * 0.5
    x = torch.randn(7, H)

    def build(**kw):
        cfg = BranchConfig(seed=0, **kw)
        return BranchMoEFFN(gate.clone(), up.clone(), down.clone(), cfg)

    checks: list[tuple[str, bool, str]] = []

    def check(name, ok, detail=""):
        checks.append((name, bool(ok), detail))

    # 1. A tiled cut that keeps every tile IS the dense layer.
    m = build(n_experts=4, top_k=4, init="tile", expert_units=I // 4,
              combine="sum")
    check("tile k=E is exact", m.residual_report(x)["residual_all_experts_sum"]
          < 1e-5)
    check("tile k=1 loses output",
          m.residual_report(x)["residual_best_single_expert"] > 0.5)

    # 2. A full-width replica at k=1 reproduces the dense layer exactly, while
    #    a narrow replica cannot -- the router has one block and no choice.
    full = build(n_experts=3, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=I,
                 combine="sum")
    rep = full.residual_report(x)
    check("replica k=1 full width is exact", rep["residual_router"] < 1e-5,
          f"router={rep['residual_router']:.2e}")
    check("replica full is not sparse", full.active_width_fraction == 1.0)
    narrow = build(n_experts=3, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=I // 4,
                   combine="sum")
    check("narrow replica loses output",
          narrow.residual_report(x)["residual_router"] > 0.5)
    check("narrow replica matches its width",
          abs(narrow.active_width_fraction - 0.25) < 1e-9)

    # 3. Storage scales with E; active width does not.
    check("stored grows with E", build(n_experts=8, top_k=1, init="replica",
                                       expert_units=I).stored_params
          > build(n_experts=2, top_k=1, init="replica",
                  expert_units=I).stored_params)

    # 4. A shared expert adds width without taking a routing slot.
    sh = build(n_experts=3, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=I // 2,
               n_shared=1, shared_units=I // 2, combine="softmax")
    check("shared expert contributes", "residual_shared_only"
          in sh.residual_report(x))

    # 5. The residual_report keys are all present and finite for both inits.
    for name, mod in (("tile", m), ("replica", full)):
        r = mod.residual_report(x)
        need = {"residual_best_single_expert", "residual_oracle_topk",
                "residual_router", "residual_all_experts_sum",
                "active_width_fraction", "stored_width_fraction"}
        check(f"{name} report complete", need <= set(r))
        check(f"{name} report finite",
              all(v == v and abs(v) < 1e6 for v in r.values()))

    # 6. `force_expert` bypasses the router (the stage-2 mechanism). At full
    #    width it must reproduce the dense layer, and it must be clearable.
    fr = build(n_experts=4, top_k=1, init="replica", expert_units=I,
               combine="softmax")
    fr.set_force_expert(2)
    check("force_expert reproduces dense",
          float((fr(x) - fr.dense_forward(x)).norm()) < 1e-5)
    check("force_expert is recorded", fr.last_stats.get("forced_expert") == 2)
    check("force_expert suppresses aux loss",
          float(fr.last_aux_loss) == 0.0)
    fr.set_force_expert(None)
    check("force_expert clears", fr.force_expert is None)
    try:
        fr.set_force_expert(9)
        check("force_expert range-checked", False)
    except ValueError:
        check("force_expert range-checked", True)

    # 7. A short training loop runs end to end on synthetic tokens.
    class _Tiny(nn.Module):
        def __init__(self):
            super().__init__()
            self.model = nn.Module()
            lay = nn.Module()
            lay.mlp = BranchMoEFFN(gate.clone(), up.clone(), down.clone(),
                                   BranchConfig(n_experts=2, top_k=1,
                                                init="replica",
                                                expert_units=I // 2,
                                                combine="softmax", seed=0))
            self.model.layers = nn.ModuleList([lay])
            self.head = nn.Linear(H, 3)

        def forward(self, idx, labels=None):
            h = self.model.layers[0].mlp(idx.float().mean(-1, keepdim=True)
                                         .expand(-1, H))
            logits = self.head(h)
            loss = F.cross_entropy(logits, labels) if labels is not None else None
            return type("O", (), {"loss": loss})()

    tiny = _Tiny()
    moe = tiny.model.layers[0].mlp
    before = moe.router.weight.detach().clone()
    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(moe.parameters(), lr=1e-3)
    blocks = [(torch.randint(0, 3, (4, 8)), torch.randint(0, 3, (4,)))]
    for _ in range(3):
        opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
        out = tiny(blocks[0][0], labels=blocks[0][1])
        (out.loss + moe.total_aux_loss()).backward()
        opt.step()
    moved = not torch.equal(before, moe.router.weight.detach())
    check("router receives gradient under softmax", moved)

    fails = [c for c in checks if not c[1]]
    for name, ok, detail in checks:
        print(f"  [{'ok ' if ok else 'FAIL'}] {name}"
              + (f"  ({detail})" if detail and not ok else ""))
    print(f"self-test: {len(checks) - len(fails)}/{len(checks)} passed")
    return 1 if fails else 0


def build_args(argv=None):
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description=__doc__,
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument("--model", default="Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B")
    ap.add_argument("--layers", nargs="+", default=["0", "3", "8", "23"])
    ap.add_argument("--steps", type=int, default=150)
    ap.add_argument("--lr", type=float, default=1e-4)
    ap.add_argument("--weight-decay", type=float, default=0.0)
    ap.add_argument("--aux-loss-coef", type=float, default=1e-2)
    ap.add_argument("--clip", type=float, default=1.0)
    ap.add_argument("--seq-len", type=int, default=512)
    ap.add_argument("--grad-accum", type=int, default=4)
    ap.add_argument("--eval-blocks", type=int, default=4)
    ap.add_argument("--corpus-chars", type=int, default=4_000_000)
    ap.add_argument("--dataset", default="HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu")
    ap.add_argument("--text-file", default=None)
    ap.add_argument("--offline", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=0)
    ap.add_argument("--device", default=None)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="results/branch_poc.json")
    ap.add_argument("--btx", action="store_true",
                    help="add the branch-train-miX configs: each expert is "
                         "trained alone on its own domain, then a router is "
                         "learned on top")
    ap.add_argument("--branch-steps", type=int, default=40,
                    help="stage-2 steps per expert (independent training)")
    ap.add_argument("--domain-chars", type=int, default=600_000,
                    help="characters of each domain stream for stage 2")
    ap.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16",
                    choices=["bfloat16", "float16", "float32"],
                    help="weight dtype. float32 makes the exactness identities "
                         "(tile k=E, replica k=1) hold to ~1e-6 instead of the "
                         "bf16 rounding floor; use it on a model that fits.")
    ap.add_argument("--only", nargs="+", default=None,
                    help="run only the configs whose name contains one of these "
                         "substrings; everything else is left untouched. Use "
                         "with --resume to finish a run that died part-way "
                         "without recomputing the ladder that already passed.")
    ap.add_argument("--resume", action="store_true",
                    help="reuse the configs already present in --out, skipping "
                         "the ones that completed cleanly")
    ap.add_argument("--self-test", action="store_true",
                    help="offline CPU check of the module and the plumbing")
    return ap.parse_args(argv)


def main() -> int:
    args = build_args()
    if args.self_test:
        return self_test()
    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer

    torch.manual_seed(args.seed)
    # The whole study compares residuals of a *sum* against the sum itself. On
    # CUDA, TF32 leaves a ~5e-3 relative floor on every matmul, which is the
    # same size as the differences being reported (tile vs replica at equal
    # width differs by ~0.03). Turn it off so an "exact" identity reads as
    # exact and a small difference is architectural rather than arithmetic.
    torch.backends.cuda.matmul.allow_tf32 = False
    torch.backends.cudnn.allow_tf32 = False
    device = torch.device(args.device or ("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available()
                                          else "cpu"))
    amp_dtype = POC.pick_amp_dtype(device)
    scaler = POC.make_grad_scaler(amp_dtype)
    print(f"device={device} amp={amp_dtype} model={args.model}")

    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model)
    wdtype = getattr(torch, args.dtype)
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        args.model, dtype=wdtype).to(device)
    model.eval()

    ids, corpus = POC.load_token_stream(args, tokenizer)
    blocks = POC.make_blocks(ids, args.seq_len)
    n_val = max(args.eval_blocks, 2)
    train_blocks = blocks[:-n_val]
    val_blocks = blocks[-n_val:]
    print(f"blocks: {len(train_blocks)} train / {len(val_blocks)} val")

    # Stage-2 data: one token stream per domain. Held-out blocks from each
    # domain are kept so specialisation can be *measured* per domain rather
    # than only inferred from the pooled validation loss.
    domain_train, domain_val = [], []
    if args.btx:
        print("domains (one per branch):")
        for label, ds, cfg_name, split, field in DOMAINS:
            text = load_domain_text(label, ds, cfg_name, split, field,
                                    args.domain_chars)
            if not text:
                continue
            dids = tokenizer(text, return_tensors="pt").input_ids[0]
            dblocks = POC.make_blocks(dids, args.seq_len)
            if len(dblocks) < 3:
                print(f"  domain {label}: only {len(dblocks)} blocks, skipped")
                continue
            domain_train.append(dblocks[:-2])
            domain_val.append({"domain": label, "blocks": dblocks[-2:]})
        print(f"domains usable: {len(domain_train)}")

    layers = [int(v) for v in args.layers]
    reference = POC.capture_reference(model, val_blocks[:1], layers, device)
    dense_ppl = POC.eval_perplexity(model, val_blocks, device, amp_dtype,
                                    args.eval_blocks)
    print(f"dense val ppl {dense_ppl:.4f}")

    inter = int(model.model.layers[layers[0]].mlp.gate_proj.weight.shape[0])
    print(f"intermediate width {inter}\n")

    originals = [(i, model.model.layers[i].mlp) for i in layers]
    blocks_mod = model.model.layers

    runs, report = [], {
        "model": args.model, "device": str(device),
        "weight_dtype": args.dtype,
        "amp_dtype": str(amp_dtype), "corpus": corpus,
        "corpus_tokens": int(ids.numel()), "seq_len": args.seq_len,
        "dense_val_ppl": dense_ppl, "layers": layers,
        "intermediate": inter,
    }

    specs = config_table(inter)
    if args.btx and domain_train:
        specs += btx_table(inter, len(domain_train))

    # Resume: a run that is killed part-way (OOM, session cap) has usually
    # finished the cheap configurations and died on an expensive one. Reloading
    # the completed entries means the expensive part can be retried alone.
    done: dict[str, dict] = {}
    if args.resume and os.path.exists(args.out):
        with open(args.out) as fh:
            prev = json.load(fh)
        for e in prev.get("runs", []):
            if e.get("name") and not e.get("error"):
                done[e["name"]] = e
        runs = [done[s["name"]] for s in specs if s["name"] in done]
        report = {**report, **{k: v for k, v in prev.items()
                               if k not in ("runs",)}}
        print(f"resume: {len(done)} configs already complete "
              f"({', '.join(sorted(done))})")
    if args.only:
        specs = [s for s in specs
                 if any(sub in s["name"] for sub in args.only)]
        print(f"only: {len(specs)} configs selected")

    for spec in specs:
        if args.resume and spec["name"] in done:
            print(f"=== {spec['name']}: already complete, skipped ===")
            continue
        name = spec["name"]
        cfg = BranchConfig(combine=spec["combine"], seed=args.seed, **spec["cfg"])
        print(f"=== {name}  combine={cfg.combine} "
              f"E={cfg.n_experts} k={cfg.top_k} "
              f"units={cfg.expert_units} shared={cfg.n_shared} ===")
        restore(blocks_mod, originals)
        try:
            moes = branchify(model, layers, cfg)
            before = residual_across_layers(moes, reference, device)
            train_stats = None
            btx_stats = None
            after_stage2 = None
            if spec.get("btx"):
                btx_stats = branch_train_experts(model, moes, args, domain_train,
                                                 device, amp_dtype, scaler)
                after_stage2 = residual_across_layers(moes, reference, device)
            if spec["train"]:
                train_stats = train_model(model, moes, args, train_blocks,
                                          device, amp_dtype, scaler)
                after = residual_across_layers(moes, reference, device)
            else:
                after = before
            ppl = POC.eval_perplexity(model, val_blocks, device, amp_dtype,
                                     args.eval_blocks)
            entry = {
                "name": name,
                "config": {"n_experts": cfg.n_experts, "top_k": cfg.top_k,
                           "init": cfg.init, "expert_units": cfg.expert_units,
                           "n_shared": cfg.n_shared, "combine": cfg.combine},
                "accounting": accounting(moes),
                "untrained": before,
                "trained": after if train_stats else None,
                "train": train_stats,
                "val_ppl": ppl,
                "routing": {i: m.last_stats for i, m in moes},
                "error": None,
            }
            if btx_stats is not None:
                entry["btx"] = btx_stats
                entry["btx"]["residual_after_stage2"] = after_stage2
                entry["domain_ppl"] = {
                    d["domain"]: POC.eval_perplexity(
                        model, d["blocks"], device, amp_dtype, len(d["blocks"]))
                    for d in domain_val
                }
                # Per-expert usage on a shared batch: a specialised router
                # should spread across experts, not collapse onto one.
                entry["expert_usage_on_val"] = {
                    i: m.last_stats.get("usage_fraction")
                    for i, m in moes
                }
            print(f"    width {before['active_width_fraction']:.3f} "
                  f"stored {before['stored_width_fraction']:.2f}x | "
                  f"best1 {before['residual_best_single_expert']:.4f} "
                  f"oracle {before['residual_oracle_topk']:.4f} "
                  f"router {before['residual_router']:.4f} "
                  f"all {before['residual_all_experts_sum']:.4f} "
                  f"| ppl {ppl:.2f}")
            if train_stats:
                print(f"    after {train_stats['steps_completed']} steps: "
                      f"best1 {after['residual_best_single_expert']:.4f} "
                      f"router {after['residual_router']:.4f} | ppl {ppl:.2f}")
        except Exception as e:                                  # noqa: BLE001
            print(f"    FAILED: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
            entry = {"name": name, "error": f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}"}
        runs.append(entry)
        report["runs"] = runs
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.out) or ".", exist_ok=True)
        with open(args.out, "w") as fh:
            json.dump(report, fh, indent=2)
        if device.type == "cuda":
            torch.cuda.empty_cache()

    restore(blocks_mod, originals)
    print(f"\nwrote {args.out}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())


===== experiments/eval_gate.py =====

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
eval_gate.py -- reproducible evaluation set with executable checks.

Purpose: give `docs/decision_memo.md` Path A a real gate. "Q3 still feels fine"
is not a gate; this prints pass/fail per task category so the quantisation
ladder decision can be made on evidence.

Categories
----------
  coding       generated function is EXECUTED against fixed cases
  reasoning    numeric answer extracted and compared
  format       output must parse as JSON with the required keys/types
  instruction  explicit constraint compliance (counts, casing, exact tokens)

Design notes
------------
* Deterministic: temperature 0, fixed seed, fixed num_predict cap.
* Executable correctness, not string matching, for coding tasks.
* Failures are reported individually. Aggregate scores never hide a brittle
  failure: every task keeps its id, category, extracted answer and reason.

SAFETY: coding tasks execute model-generated Python in a subprocess with a
timeout, in a temporary directory, with the parent process's environment. That
is the point of the harness, but it does mean you are running code a local model
wrote. Use --no-execute to skip execution if you do not want that.

Scope: this is a STARTER set (30 tasks). The handoff specifies 30 coding /
30 reasoning / 20 instruction+tool / 20 chat plus context tests, ideally with
respected public benchmarks and contamination checks. This harness is the
scaffold and the plumbing those need; extend TASKS and add a contamination
check before treating any score as publishable.

Usage:
    python3 experiments/eval_gate.py --model qwen3:4b
    python3 experiments/eval_gate.py --model A --model B --out results/eval.json
    python3 experiments/eval_gate.py --model X --no-execute
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import time
import urllib.request

# Thinking models (qwen3, qwen3.5, qwen3.8) spend an unbounded number of tokens
# in a separate `thinking` field before emitting `response`. With too small a
# cap they truncate mid-reasoning and return an EMPTY answer, which must not be
# silently scored as a capability failure. 512 is not enough; 1536 is the
# default here and truncations are reported explicitly.
#
# The first version of this only labelled truncation when the answer text was
# EMPTY. A capped model that narrates before it answers -- every verbose model at
# a small cap -- then recorded a bare "got 3.0, want 42", which reads as a wrong
# answer rather than a run the harness cut off. On the 19-task subset that
# mis-scored 3 of 4 reasoning failures of the dense 27B, all of which were
# truncated mid-solution on the way to the right number. Rows now carry
# `truncated`, and both scores are printed: the strict one (every row counts)
# and the one with capped rows removed.
API = "http://127.0.0.1:11434"
# llama.cpp `llama-server` speaks a different request shape. Same task set, same
# scorer, same greedy decode -- only the engine differs -- is what makes an
# engine comparison (surplus's placement, or llama.cpp against the ollama tag)
# mean anything, and it doubles as an agreement check: identical prompts at
# temperature 0 should give identical answers if the weights and the graph are
# the same. Start it with `llama-server -m <gguf> --port 8080 ...`.
LLAMA_API = "http://127.0.0.1:8080"
NUM_PREDICT = 1536
SEED = 1234

# Two expected values in this set were arithmetically wrong, in OPPOSITE
# directions, so they were scoring correct answers as failures and failures as
# passes (both are pinned by tests/test_eval_gate.py):
#
#   r_bat_ball  was expect=0.10. ball = (1.10 - 1.00) / 2 = 0.05.
#   r_modular   was expect=1.    7**20 % 13 = 3  (7**12 % 13 == 1, 20 % 12 == 8,
#                                and 7**8 % 13 == 3).
#
# `--rescore` replays the recorded responses through the corrected definitions,
# so runs made before this fix are re-scored from their stored text instead of
# being re-generated.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# task set: every check is mechanical
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _t(tid, cat, prompt, check, **kw):
    d = {"id": tid, "category": cat, "prompt": prompt, "check": check}
    d.update(kw)
    return d


TASKS = [
    # ---------------- coding: executed against fixed cases ----------------
    _t("c_sum_evens", "coding",
       "Write a Python function `solve(nums)` that returns the sum of the even "
       "numbers in the list `nums`. Reply with a single ```python code block only.",
       "code", cases=[([[1, 2, 3, 4]], 6), ([[]], 0), ([[2, 2, 5]], 4), ([[-2, 3]], -2)]),
    _t("c_reverse_words", "coding",
       "Write a Python function `solve(s)` that reverses the order of words in the "

       "```python code block only.",
       "code", cases=[(["hello world"], "world hello"), (["a"], "a"),

       "code", cases=[([5], ["1", "2", "Fizz", "4", "Buzz"]),

    _t("c_is_palindrome", "coding",

       "code", cases=[([[2, 7, 11, 15], 9], [0, 1]), ([[3, 3], 6], [0, 1]),

    _t("c_word_count", "coding",
       "Write a Python function `solve(text)` returning a dict mapping each "

       "single ```python code block only.",
       "code", cases=[(["a b a"], {"a": 2, "b": 1}), ([""], {})]),

    _t("r_train", "reasoning",

       "numeric_time", expect="16:50"),

       "A bat and a ball cost 1.10 in total. The bat costs 1.00 more than the "
       "ball. How much does the ball cost, in currency units? End with "
       "'ANSWER: <value>'.",
       # ball + (ball + 1.00) = 1.10 -> ball = 0.05. This previously read 0.10,

       "starts_with", ch="c"),

       "Write a short sentence about binary trees that does NOT contain the "

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# extraction helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def extract_code(text: str) -> str:
    m = re.search(r"```(?:python|py)?\s*\n(.*?)```", text, re.S)
    if m:
        return m.group(1).strip()
    m = re.search(r"(^|\n)(def solve\(.*)", text, re.S)
    return m.group(2).strip() if m else ""


def extract_json(text: str) -> tuple[object | None, str]:
    m = re.search(r"```(?:json)?\s*\n(.*?)```", text, re.S)
    body = m.group(1).strip() if m else text.strip()
    # try whole body first, then scan for the first balanced {...} or [...]
    for candidate in (body, _first_balanced(body, "{", "}"), _first_balanced(body, "[", "]")):
        if not candidate:
            continue
        try:
            return json.loads(candidate), ""
        except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
            last = str(e)
    return None, "no parseable JSON found"


def _first_balanced(s: str, open_ch: str, close_ch: str) -> str:
    start = s.find(open_ch)
    while start != -1:
        depth, in_str, esc = 0, False, False
        for i in range(start, len(s)):
            c = s[i]
            if in_str:
                if esc:
                    esc = False
                elif c == "\\":
                    esc = True
                elif c == '"':
                    in_str = False
                continue
            if c == '"':
                in_str = True
            elif c == open_ch:
                depth += 1
            elif c == close_ch:
                depth -= 1
                if depth == 0:
                    return s[start:i + 1]
        start = s.find(open_ch, start + 1)
    return ""


def extract_number(text: str):
    m = re.findall(r"ANSWER:\s*(-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?)", text, re.I)
    if m:
        return float(m[-1])
    nums = re.findall(r"-?\d+(?:\.\d+)?", text.replace(",", ""))
    return float(nums[-1]) if nums else None


def extract_time(text: str):
    m = re.findall(r"\b([01]?\d|2[0-3]):([0-5]\d)\b", text)
    return f"{int(m[-1][0]):02d}:{m[-1][1]}" if m else None


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# checks
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_code(code: str, cases: list, timeout: float = 10.0) -> tuple[bool, str]:
    """Execute model-generated code against cases in a subprocess.

    The candidate source is concatenated at column 0 rather than interpolated
    into an indented template: a template plus textwrap.dedent collapses the
    function body's indentation and yields IndentationError on every correct
    solution, which silently makes the whole coding category fail.
    """
    driver = (
        "import json, sys\n"
        f"{code}\n"
        "cases = json.loads(sys.argv[1])\n"
        "results = []\n"
        "for args, expected in cases:\n"
        "    try:\n"
        "        got = solve(*args)\n"
        "    except Exception as e:\n"
        "        results.append(['error', type(e).__name__ + ': ' + str(e)])\n"
        "        continue\n"
        "    results.append(['ok', got == expected])\n"
        "print(json.dumps(results))\n"
    )
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="moeme_eval_") as d:
        path = os.path.join(d, "candidate.py")
        with open(path, "w") as fh:
            fh.write(driver)
        try:
            p = subprocess.run(
                [sys.executable, path, json.dumps(cases)],
                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=timeout, cwd=d,
            )
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            return False, "timeout"
    if p.returncode != 0:
        return False, f"runtime error: {(p.stderr or '').strip().splitlines()[-1][:120]}"
    try:
        res = json.loads(p.stdout.strip().splitlines()[-1])
    except Exception:
        return False, "could not parse driver output"
    bad = [r for r in res if r[0] != "ok" or r[1] is not True]
    if bad:
        # Surface the exception type: "1/1 cases failed" alone is not enough to
        # tell a wrong answer from a crash.
        errs = [r[1] for r in res if r[0] == "error"]
        if errs:
            return False, f"{len(bad)}/{len(res)} cases failed (e.g. {str(errs[0])[:100]})"
        return False, f"{len(bad)}/{len(res)} cases failed"
    return True, f"{len(res)}/{len(res)} cases passed"


def check_json(obj, task) -> tuple[bool, str]:
    if obj is None:
        return False, "no JSON"
    if task.get("json_root") is list and not isinstance(obj, list):
        return False, "root is not an array"
    if task.get("json_root") is list:
        if "array_len" in task and len(obj) != task["array_len"]:
            return False, f"array length {len(obj)} != {task['array_len']}"
        for item in obj:
            for k in task.get("item_keys", []):
                if not isinstance(item, dict) or k not in item:
                    return False, f"item missing key {k!r}"
        return True, "ok"
    def type_ok(value, typ) -> bool:
        # bool subclasses int in Python, so a JSON `true` would otherwise
        # validate against an integer field. Reject that explicitly.
        if typ in (int, float) and isinstance(value, bool):
            return False
        return isinstance(value, typ)

    for key, typ in task.get("schema", {}).items():
        if key not in obj:
            return False, f"missing key {key!r}"
        if not type_ok(obj[key], typ):
            return False, f"{key!r} is {type(obj[key]).__name__}, want {typ.__name__}"
    for parent, sub in task.get("nested", {}).items():
        for k, typ in sub.items():
            if k not in obj.get(parent, {}) or not type_ok(obj[parent][k], typ):
                return False, f"{parent}.{k} wrong type/missing"
    for key, n in task.get("list_len", {}).items():
        if len(obj.get(key, [])) != n:
            return False, f"{key!r} has {len(obj.get(key, []))} items, want {n}"
    return True, "ok"


def evaluate(task, text: str, execute: bool) -> tuple[bool, str]:
    c = task["check"]
    if c == "code":
        code = extract_code(text)
        if "def solve" not in code:
            return False, "no solve() function found"
        if not execute:
            return False, "execution disabled (--no-execute)"
        return run_code(code, task["cases"])
    if c == "number":
        v = extract_number(text)
        if v is None:
            return False, "no number found"
        return (abs(v - task["expect"]) <= task["tol"], f"got {v}, want {task['expect']}")
    if c == "numeric_time":
        v = extract_time(text)
        return (v == task["expect"], f"got {v}, want {task['expect']}")
    if c == "json":
        return check_json(extract_json(text)[0], task)
    if c == "exact":
        got = text.strip()
        return (got == task["expect"], f"got {got[:40]!r}")
    if c == "bullets":
        lines = [l for l in text.strip().splitlines() if l.strip().startswith("- ")]
        return (len(lines) == task["n"], f"{len(lines)} bullets, want {task['n']}")
    if c == "word_count":
        n = len(text.split())
        return (n == task["n"], f"{n} words, want {task['n']}")
    if c == "starts_with":
        s = text.strip()
        return (s[:1] == task["ch"], f"starts with {s[:1]!r}")
    if c == "forbid_char":
        present = task["ch"].lower() in text.lower()
        return (not present,
                f"contains {task['ch']!r}" if present else f"no {task['ch']!r}")
    raise ValueError(f"unknown check {c}")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# runner
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def server_up() -> bool:
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{API}/api/version", timeout=5) as r:
            json.load(r)
        return True
    except Exception:
        return False


def ensure_server() -> bool:
    if server_up():
        return True
    print("starting ollama serve ...", flush=True)
    subprocess.Popen(["ollama", "serve"], stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL,
                     stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL, start_new_session=True)
    for _ in range(40):
        time.sleep(0.5)
        if server_up():
            return True
    return False


def mark_truncation(detail: str, text: str, done_reason,
                    eval_count) -> tuple[str, bool]:
    """Label a row the token cap cut off, so it is not read as a wrong answer.

    `detail` is the scorer's verdict and is kept: a truncated row still holds no
    usable answer, so it stays a failure in the strict score. The marker and the
    returned flag are what let a reader (and the summary) separate "the model
    could not do this" from "the harness stopped it mid-sentence".
    """
    if done_reason != "length":
        return detail, False
    if not text.strip():
        return (f"TRUNCATED during reasoning ({eval_count} tokens, empty answer; "
                f"raise --num-predict or use --think off)"), True
    return f"{detail} [truncated at {eval_count} tokens]", True


def row_is_truncated(row: dict) -> bool:
    """True for rows that hit the cap, including reports written before the
    `truncated` field existed (they are marked only in `detail`)."""
    return bool(row.get("truncated")) or "TRUNCATED" in (row.get("detail") or "")


def ask_llama(prompt: str, num_predict: int = NUM_PREDICT, *,
              api: str = LLAMA_API, timeout: float = 3600.0,
              template_kwargs: dict | None = None) -> tuple[str, dict]:
    """One greedy completion from a llama.cpp `llama-server`.

    Via /v1/chat/completions, not /completion, and that is the whole point.
    /completion is a raw continuation: it applies no chat template, so a
    thinking model starts from a bare instruction and writes its `<think>` block
    into the answer. Measured here, that alone cost a placement's score 13/19
    against 17/19 on identical weights and prompts -- a template default
    reported as engine quality. The chat route applies the model's template, so
    `chat_template_kwargs={"enable_thinking": false}` reproduces what the ollama
    runs get from `think: false`, and a bare `yes` comes back instead of
    `<think>...why 17 is prime...</think>\nyes`.

    Same returned shape as `ask`, so extraction, scoring, truncation labelling
    and checkpointing are shared rather than reimplemented per engine. llama.cpp
    reports a cap hit as finish_reason "length" (or `stopped_limit`) where ollama
    says `done_reason == "length"`; both map onto the one condition
    `mark_truncation` understands.
    """
    payload = {"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
               "n_predict": num_predict, "temperature": 0.0, "seed": SEED,
               "stream": False}
    if template_kwargs:
        payload["chat_template_kwargs"] = template_kwargs
    req = urllib.request.Request(f"{api}/v1/chat/completions",
                                 data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as r:
        resp = json.load(r)
    choice = (resp.get("choices") or [{}])[0]
    message = choice.get("message") or {}
    # A build that ignores the kwarg still answers, just not in `content`.
    # Flagging it beats either scoring an empty answer as a wrong one or
    # quietly scoring the reasoning as the answer.
    reasoning = message.get("reasoning_content") or ""
    capped = (choice.get("finish_reason") == "length"
              or bool(resp.get("stopped_limit")))
    timings = resp.get("timings") or {}
    return message.get("content") or "", {
        "eval_count": timings.get("predicted_n"),
        "decode_tok_s": timings.get("predicted_per_second"),
        "prompt_tok_s": timings.get("prompt_per_second"),
        "done_reason": "length" if capped else "stop",
        "thinking_chars": len(reasoning),
        "reasoning_only": bool(reasoning) and not (message.get("content") or ""),
        "num_predict": num_predict,
    }


def ask_backend(backend: str, model: str, prompt: str, num_predict: int, *,
                think: str, api: str, keep_alive: str, timeout: float,
                template_kwargs: dict | None = None) -> tuple[str, dict]:
    if backend == "llama":
        return ask_llama(prompt, num_predict, api=api, timeout=timeout,
                         template_kwargs=template_kwargs)
    return ask(model, prompt, num_predict, think=think, api=api,
               keep_alive=keep_alive, timeout=timeout)


def ask_llama_ready(api: str) -> bool:
    """llama-server answers /health with 503 while it is still loading weights,
    which is not the same thing as a wrong URL, but either way the run cannot
    start yet."""
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{api}/health", timeout=3) as r:
            return r.status == 200
    except Exception:
        return False


def ask(model: str, prompt: str, num_predict: int = NUM_PREDICT, *,
        think: str = "auto", api: str = API, keep_alive: str = "30s",
        timeout: float = 1800.0) -> tuple[str, dict]:
    payload = {"model": model, "prompt": prompt, "stream": False,
               "keep_alive": keep_alive,
               "options": {"temperature": 0.0, "seed": SEED,
                           "num_predict": num_predict}}
    # Only send `think` when asked for a specific behaviour. Leaving the key out
    # keeps the model's own default, which is what --think auto means, and it
    # avoids a hard error on builds that predate the parameter.
    if think == "off":
        payload["think"] = False
    elif think == "on":
        payload["think"] = True
    req = urllib.request.Request(f"{api}/api/generate",
                                 data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
                                 headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as r:
        resp = json.load(r)
    ec, ed = resp.get("eval_count") or 0, resp.get("eval_duration") or 0
    pc, pd = resp.get("prompt_eval_count") or 0, resp.get("prompt_eval_duration") or 0
    return resp.get("response", ""), {
        "eval_count": ec,
        "decode_tok_s": (ec / (ed / 1e9)) if ed else None,
        # Prompt-processing rate on the same terms as the llama.cpp backend, so
        # prefill can be compared between engines instead of only between a run
        # and a separate 32-token benchmark.
        "prompt_tok_s": (pc / (pd / 1e9)) if pd else None,
        "done_reason": resp.get("done_reason"),
        "thinking_chars": len(resp.get("thinking") or ""),
        "num_predict": num_predict,
    }


def rescore(args) -> int:
    """Re-run the current task definitions over the RECORDED responses.

    Model answers are stored verbatim, so a corrected expectation (or a scorer
    fix) can be applied to an existing multi-hour run without re-issuing a single
    prompt. Rows recorded before responses were stored are left exactly as they
    were and counted in the output, never silently turned into failures.
    """
    if not os.path.exists(args.out):
        raise SystemExit(f"--rescore needs an existing --out, and {args.out} does not exist")
    with open(args.out) as fh:
        report = json.load(fh)
    if "task_ids" not in report:
        raise SystemExit(f"{args.out} predates --tasks, so there is no task_ids "
                         f"list to re-score against")
    by_id = {t["id"]: t for t in TASKS}
    changed = unchanged = skipped = 0
    for model, m in sorted(report.get("models", {}).items()):
        by_cat: dict[str, list] = {}
        for r in m.get("tasks", []):
            response, task = r.get("response"), by_id.get(r["id"])
            if response is None or task is None:
                skipped += 1
            else:
                ok, detail = evaluate(task, response, execute=not args.no_execute)
                # Truncation is a property of the recorded run, not of the
                # definition, so it is re-derived from the stored done_reason.
                # Reviews a report the harness wrote before it tracked this.
                detail, r["truncated"] = mark_truncation(
                    detail, response, r.get("done_reason"),
                    r.get("eval_count") or r.get("response_chars"))
                if ok != r["passed"]:
                    changed += 1
                    print(f"  {model} {r['id']}: {r['passed']} -> {ok}  ({detail})")
                else:
                    unchanged += 1
                r["passed"], r["detail"] = ok, detail
            by_cat.setdefault(r["category"], []).append(r["passed"])
        rows = m.get("tasks", [])
        passed = sum(1 for r in rows if r["passed"])
        m["summary"] = {c: {"passed": sum(v), "total": len(v),
                            "rate": sum(v) / len(v),
                            "truncated": sum(1 for r in rows
                                             if r["category"] == c
                                             and row_is_truncated(r))}
                        for c, v in sorted(by_cat.items())}
        m["overall"] = {"passed": passed, "total": len(rows),
                        "rate": (passed / len(rows)) if rows else 0.0}
        scored_rows = [r for r in rows if not row_is_truncated(r)]
        scored_pass = sum(1 for r in scored_rows if r["passed"])
        m["overall_untruncated"] = {
            "passed": scored_pass, "total": len(scored_rows),
            "rate": (scored_pass / len(scored_rows)) if scored_rows else 0.0}
        m["truncated_tasks"] = sum(1 for r in rows if row_is_truncated(r))
        m["harness_errors"] = sum(1 for r in rows
                                  if "harness error" in (r.get("detail") or ""))
        tail = (f", {m['truncated_tasks']} truncated ("
                f"{scored_pass}/{len(scored_rows)} excluding capped rows)"
                if m["truncated_tasks"] else "")
        print(f"  {model}: now {passed}/{len(rows)}{tail}")
    tmp = args.out + ".tmp"
    with open(tmp, "w") as fh:
        json.dump(report, fh, indent=2)
    os.replace(tmp, args.out)
    print(f"re-scored {changed + unchanged} task(s): {changed} changed, "
          f"{unchanged} unchanged, {skipped} with no stored response "
          f"(left as recorded) -> {args.out}")
    return 0


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--model", action="append", default=[])
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="results/eval.json")
    ap.add_argument("--no-execute", action="store_true",
                    help="do not run model-generated code (coding tasks auto-fail)")
    ap.add_argument("--only", default=None, help="comma-separated categories")
    ap.add_argument("--tasks", default=None,
                    help="comma-separated task ids -- a fixed subset, for a model "
                         "too slow to run the full set in one sitting")
    ap.add_argument("--num-predict", type=int, default=NUM_PREDICT)
    ap.add_argument("--think", choices=("auto", "off", "on"), default="auto",
                    help="thinking models: 'off' suppresses the separate reasoning "
                         "field, which is what makes a small --num-predict usable. "
                         "'auto' sends nothing and keeps the model default")
    ap.add_argument("--chat-template-kwargs", default=None,
                    help="JSON passed to a llama.cpp server as "
                         "chat_template_kwargs, e.g. '{\"enable_thinking\": false}'. "
                         "Use it to match --think off: the server's template "
                         "otherwise leaves a thinking model's <think> block in "
                         "the answer, which fails strict-instruction tasks")
    ap.add_argument("--backend", choices=("ollama", "llama"), default="ollama",
                    help="ollama /api/generate (default) or a llama.cpp "
                         "llama-server POST /completion, for comparing engines on "
                         "the identical task set")
    ap.add_argument("--api", default=None, help="base URL of the backend")
    ap.add_argument("--keep-alive", default="30s",
                    help="how long the model stays resident between tasks "
                         "(reloading the dense 27B costs ~51 s)")
    ap.add_argument("--timeout", type=float, default=1800.0,
                    help="per-request HTTP timeout in seconds")
    ap.add_argument("--resume", action="store_true",
                    help="reuse tasks already recorded in --out and continue from there")
    ap.add_argument("--pidfile", default=None,
                    help="write this process's PID here at startup, so a memory "
                         "watchdog (tools/mem_watchdog.py) can guard this run. "
                         "Without it the guard has to be aimed by hand at a "
                         "wrapper process, which is how a guard ends up armed on "
                         "the wrong PID.")
    ap.add_argument("--rescore", action="store_true",
                    help="do not run a model: re-evaluate the responses already "
                         "stored in --out against the current task definitions")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    if args.rescore:
        return rescore(args)
    if not args.model:
        raise SystemExit("give at least one --model")
    if args.pidfile:
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.pidfile) or ".", exist_ok=True)
        with open(args.pidfile, "w") as fh:
            fh.write(str(os.getpid()))
        print(f"NOTE: pid {os.getpid()} written to {args.pidfile}", flush=True)
    api = args.api or (LLAMA_API if args.backend == "llama" else API)
    template_kwargs = None
    if args.chat_template_kwargs:
        if args.backend != "llama":
            raise SystemExit("--chat-template-kwargs only applies to --backend llama")
        try:
            template_kwargs = json.loads(args.chat_template_kwargs)
        except ValueError as e:
            raise SystemExit(f"--chat-template-kwargs is not valid JSON: {e}")
        if not isinstance(template_kwargs, dict):
            raise SystemExit("--chat-template-kwargs must be a JSON object")
        print(f"NOTE: sending chat_template_kwargs={template_kwargs}\n")
    if args.backend == "llama":
        # Do not spawn it: the whole point is to control the placement the
        # server was launched with, and a silent default launch would hide it.
        if not ask_llama_ready(api):
            raise SystemExit(
                f"no llama.cpp server at {api} (or it is still loading). Start "
                f"one, then rerun --backend llama --api {api}:\n"
                f"  llama-server -m <gguf> --port 8080 -ngl 42 -ncmoe 24 -c 4096")
        print(f"NOTE: measuring llama.cpp at {api}; the placement is whatever "
              f"that server was launched with.\n")
    elif not ensure_server():
        raise SystemExit("could not reach or start the ollama server")

    tasks = TASKS
    if args.only:
        cats = {c.strip() for c in args.only.split(",")}
        tasks = [t for t in tasks if t["category"] in cats]
    if args.tasks:
        want = {t.strip() for t in args.tasks.split(",") if t.strip()}
        unknown = sorted(want - {t["id"] for t in TASKS})
        if unknown:
            raise SystemExit(f"unknown task id(s): {', '.join(unknown)}")
        # Filter in canonical TASKS order so a subset run stays comparable with
        # the full run task by task, and so --resume sees the same id list.
        tasks = [t for t in tasks if t["id"] in want]
    if not tasks:
        raise SystemExit("no tasks selected")

    if not args.no_execute:
        print("NOTE: coding tasks execute model-generated Python in a subprocess "
              "with a 10 s timeout.\n")

    report = {"tasks_total": len(tasks), "models": {},
              "task_ids": [t["id"] for t in tasks],
              "backend": args.backend, "api": api,
              "chat_template_kwargs": template_kwargs,
              "think": args.think, "num_predict": args.num_predict}
    if args.resume and os.path.exists(args.out):
        try:
            with open(args.out) as fh:
                prev = json.load(fh)
        except (OSError, ValueError) as e:
            print(f"NOTE: cannot read {args.out} ({e}); starting fresh", flush=True)
            prev = {}
        if (prev.get("task_ids") == report["task_ids"]
                and prev.get("num_predict") == args.num_predict
                and prev.get("think") == args.think
                # Reports written before --backend existed are ollama reports.
                and prev.get("backend", "ollama") == args.backend):
            report["models"] = prev.get("models", {})
            have = ", ".join(f"{m}: {len(v.get('tasks', []))}"
                             for m, v in report["models"].items()) or "nothing"
            print(f"resuming {args.out}: {have}", flush=True)
        elif prev:
            print(f"NOTE: {args.out} is a different run (task_ids / num_predict / "
                  f"think / backend differ); starting fresh", flush=True)

    def save() -> None:
        """Checkpoint after every task.

        A 30-task run on the dense 27B takes hours. Losing it to one dropped
        connection -- the exact failure the CPU-only control hit -- is not
        acceptable, so the file on disk is always a valid partial report.
        """
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.out) or ".", exist_ok=True)
        tmp = args.out + ".tmp"
        with open(tmp, "w") as fh:
            json.dump(report, fh, indent=2)
        os.replace(tmp, args.out)

    for model in args.model:
        prev_run = report["models"].get(model) or {}
        results = {r["id"]: r for r in prev_run.get("tasks", [])}
        print(f"=== {model} ({len(tasks)} tasks, {len(results)} already "
              f"recorded) ===", flush=True)
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        consecutive_errors = 0
        for i, task in enumerate(tasks, 1):
            if task["id"] in results:
                print(f"  [{i:>2}/{len(tasks)}] {task['id']:<22} SKIP  already recorded",
                      flush=True)
                continue
            try:
                text, meta = ask_backend(args.backend, model, task["prompt"],
                                         args.num_predict, think=args.think,
                                         api=api, keep_alive=args.keep_alive,
                                         timeout=args.timeout,
                                         template_kwargs=template_kwargs)
                ok, detail = evaluate(task, text, execute=not args.no_execute)
                if not ok and meta.get("reasoning_only"):
                    detail = (f"{detail} [the engine answered only in its reasoning "
                              f"field -- thinking was not suppressed for this "
                              f"request, so there is no answer to score]")
                detail, truncated = mark_truncation(
                    detail, text, meta.get("done_reason"), meta.get("eval_count"))
                consecutive_errors = 0
            except Exception as e:
                text, meta, ok, detail = "", {}, False, f"harness error: {e}"
                truncated = False
                consecutive_errors += 1
            results[task["id"]] = {
                "id": task["id"], "category": task["category"],
                "passed": ok, "detail": detail, "truncated": truncated,
                "response": text[:1200],
                "eval_count": meta.get("eval_count"),
                "decode_tok_s": meta.get("decode_tok_s"),
                "prompt_tok_s": meta.get("prompt_tok_s"),
                "done_reason": meta.get("done_reason"),
                "thinking_chars": meta.get("thinking_chars"),
                "response_chars": len(text)}
            print(f"  [{i:>2}/{len(tasks)}] {task['id']:<22} "
                  f"{'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL':<4} {detail[:60]}", flush=True)
            report["models"][model] = {
                "overall": {"passed": sum(1 for r in results.values() if r["passed"]),
                            "total": len(results)},
                "tasks": [results[t["id"]] for t in tasks if t["id"] in results],
            }
            save()
            if consecutive_errors >= 3:
                print(f"  ABORT {model}: {consecutive_errors} consecutive harness "
                      f"errors -- the backend is probably gone. Partial results are "
                      f"checkpointed in {args.out}; rerun with --resume.", flush=True)
                break

        rows = [results[t["id"]] for t in tasks if t["id"] in results]
        by_cat: dict[str, list] = {}
        for r in rows:
            by_cat.setdefault(r["category"], []).append(r["passed"])
        summary = {c: {"passed": sum(v), "total": len(v),
                       "rate": sum(v) / len(v),
                       "truncated": sum(1 for r in rows if r["category"] == c
                                        and row_is_truncated(r))}
                   for c, v in sorted(by_cat.items())}
        total_pass = sum(sum(v) for v in by_cat.values())
        truncated = sum(1 for r in rows if row_is_truncated(r))
        errors = sum(1 for r in rows if "harness error" in (r.get("detail") or ""))
        scored_rows = [r for r in rows if not row_is_truncated(r)]
        scored_pass = sum(1 for r in scored_rows if r["passed"])
        report["models"][model] = {
            "summary": summary,
            "overall": {"passed": total_pass, "total": len(rows),
                        "rate": (total_pass / len(rows)) if rows else 0.0},
            "overall_untruncated": {
                "passed": scored_pass, "total": len(scored_rows),
                "rate": (scored_pass / len(scored_rows)) if scored_rows else 0.0},
            "tasks_run": len(rows),
            "tasks_selected": len(tasks),
            "truncated_tasks": truncated,
            "harness_errors": errors,
            "num_predict": args.num_predict,
            "think": args.think,
            "wall_s": (prev_run.get("wall_s") or 0.0) + (time.perf_counter() - t0),
            "tasks": rows,
        }
        save()
        print(f"  -- {model}: {total_pass}/{len(rows)} = "
              f"{100 * total_pass / max(len(rows), 1):.1f}% overall")
        for c, s in summary.items():
            note = f"  ({s['truncated']} truncated)" if s["truncated"] else ""
            print(f"     {c:<12} {s['passed']}/{s['total']} = {100*s['rate']:.0f}%{note}")
        if truncated:
            print(f"     WARNING: {truncated}/{len(rows)} task(s) hit the "
                  f"num_predict={args.num_predict} cap; a harness setting, not a "
                  f"model result")
            print(f"     excluding capped rows: {scored_pass}/{len(scored_rows)} = "
                  f"{100 * scored_pass / max(len(scored_rows), 1):.1f}%")
        if errors:
            print(f"     WARNING: {errors} task(s) hit a harness error (backend, "
                  f"not the model)")
        print()

    save()
    print(f"wrote {args.out}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())


===== experiments/pilot_sparsity.py =====

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
pilot_sparsity.py -- pilot sparsity study: quality proxy vs active compute.

Question this answers
---------------------
If you take a REAL trained FFN and slice its intermediate axis into E experts,
how much of the layer's output can you recover by activating only k of them --
with a *perfect* router (oracle) and with a *cheap learnable* router?

If the oracle curve is bad, no router can fix it and the design must change
before any training compute is spent. That is the checkpoint this pilot exists
to be.

Model choice
------------
Qwen/Qwen3.5-2B. Its text config is `qwen3_5_text` with the same
3x(GatedDeltaNet) + 1x(GatedAttention) layer pattern as Qwen3.8-27B, so its FFN
blocks are architecturally the same family. Caveats, stated plainly:
  * it is ~14x smaller, and its FFN expansion ratio is 3.0 vs the 27B's 3.4;
  * activations are real (captured from forward passes on real text), but the
    calibration corpus is small and not a benchmark.

Metric
------
Relative L2 error of the k-expert reconstruction against the true dense layer
output. 0.0 = exact, 1.0 = no better than predicting zero.

Usage:
    python3 experiments/pilot_sparsity.py --out results/pilot_sparsity.json
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import os
import sys
import time

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "src"))

import torch

from moeme.ffn import (
    DenseFFN,
    PartitionedFFN,
    clustered_plan,
    contiguous_plan,
    contribution_concentration,
    interleaved_plan,
    oracle_topk_residual,
    random_plan,
    router_topk_residual,
    train_linear_router,
)

# Chosen because it shares the 27B's architecture exactly:
#   model_type qwen3_5_text, layer pattern 3x(GatedDeltaNet)+1x(GatedAttention),
#   and FFN expansion 3584/1024 = 3.5 vs the 27B's 17408/5120 = 3.4.
# It also fits entirely in the free VRAM, which the 2B sibling does not.
MODEL_ID = os.environ.get("MOEME_PILOT_MODEL", "Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B")
TARGET_LAYERS = [0, 3, 8, 23]     # mix of DeltaNet and full-attention layers
EXPERTS = [8, 32, 64]
TOPKS = [1, 2, 4, 8]
MAX_TOKENS = 512


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# calibration corpus
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def build_calibration_text() -> str:
    """Small, reproducible, dependency-free mixed corpus.

    Code comes from this repository's own sources (real code, no network), plus
    hand-written maths/instruction/chat passages. Deliberately NOT a benchmark:
    the pilot measures routing structure, not task quality.
    """
    here = os.path.dirname(__file__)
    parts = []
    for rel in ("src/moeme/ffn.py", "src/moeme/accounting.py",
                "tools/verify_model_geometry.py"):
        p = os.path.join(here, "..", rel)
        if os.path.exists(p):
            with open(p) as fh:
                parts.append(f"# source file {rel}\n" + fh.read())
    parts.append("""
    Problem. A train leaves at 14:20 and travels 210 km at 84 km/h. When does it
    arrive? Working: the travel time is 210 / 84 = 2.5 hours, so the arrival time
    is 14:20 + 2:30 = 16:50. Check: 84 * 2.5 = 210, which matches the distance.
    Therefore the train arrives at 16:50.

    Theorem. For any finite set S, the power set of S has exactly 2**|S| elements,
    because each element is independently either present or absent, giving 2
    choices per element and hence 2**|S| distinct subsets. This generalises to
    the cardinal arithmetic identity 2**n = sum over k of the binomial
    coefficients C(n, k).

    Instruction. Summarise the following text in one sentence. Then list three
    action items as a JSON array of strings. Respond only with valid JSON.
    {"summary": "...", "actions": ["...", "...", "..."]}

    Conversation. A user asks how to reduce memory usage when loading a large
    quantised model on a laptop. A helpful answer explains memory mapping, the
    difference between resident and active parameters, why decoding is
    bandwidth bound rather than compute bound, and why swapping to a solid state
    drive makes token generation slow but not impossible.

    Code review. The function below is correct but allocates a new list on every
