    call. Consider using a generator, or appending into a preallocated buffer, to
    avoid the repeated allocation cost in the hot loop.
    """)
    return "\n\n".join(parts)


def capture_activations(model_id: str, layers: list[int], text: str):
    """Run a real forward pass and return {layer: (tokens, hidden)} MLP inputs."""
    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer

    print(f"loading {model_id} ...", flush=True)
    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_id)
    dtype = torch.bfloat16
    last_err = None
    for kwargs in ({"dtype": dtype}, {"torch_dtype": dtype}):
        try:
            model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
                model_id, low_cpu_mem_usage=True, **kwargs
            )
            break
        except TypeError as e:      # transformers renamed torch_dtype -> dtype
            last_err = e
    else:
        raise last_err
    model.eval()
    print(f"  loaded, dtype={next(model.parameters()).dtype}, "
          f"params={sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters())/1e9:.2f}B")

    # The qwen3_5 Gated DeltaNet path dispatches to causal_conv1d, whose kernel
    # in this environment is CUDA-only (`Expected x.is_cuda() to be true`).
    # So the pilot must run on the GPU, not CPU.
    if torch.cuda.is_available():
        model = model.to("cuda")
        print(f"  moved to cuda; vram allocated "
              f"{torch.cuda.memory_allocated()/1e9:.2f} GB")
    else:
        raise SystemExit(
            "no CUDA device: the qwen3_5 GatedDeltaNet path needs causal_conv1d, "
            "which is CUDA-only here. Run on the GPU, or use a qwen3 model "
            "(standard attention) instead."
        )

    blocks = model.model.layers
    caps: dict[int, list] = {i: [] for i in layers}

    def hook(i):
        def fn(_module, args):
            caps[i].append(args[0].detach().reshape(-1, args[0].shape[-1]).float())
        return fn

    handles = [blocks[i].mlp.register_forward_pre_hook(hook(i)) for i in layers
               if i < len(blocks)]
    ids = tok(text, return_tensors="pt", truncation=True,
              max_length=MAX_TOKENS).input_ids.to(model.device)
    print(f"  forward pass over {ids.shape[1]} tokens ...", flush=True)
    t0 = time.perf_counter()
    with torch.no_grad():
        model(ids)
    print(f"  done in {time.perf_counter() - t0:.1f}s")
    for h in handles:
        h.remove()

    return {i: torch.cat(v, dim=0) for i, v in caps.items() if v}, model


def ffn_from_module(model, layer: int) -> DenseFFN:
    m = model.model.layers[layer].mlp
    return DenseFFN(
        gate=m.gate_proj.weight.detach().float(),
        up=m.up_proj.weight.detach().float(),
        down=m.down_proj.weight.detach().float(),
    )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

PLANS = {
    # name: (factory, expert counts)
    "contiguous": (lambda ffn, E: contiguous_plan(ffn.intermediate, E), EXPERTS),
    "interleaved": (lambda ffn, E: interleaved_plan(ffn.intermediate, E), EXPERTS),
    "random": (lambda ffn, E: random_plan(ffn.intermediate, E, seed=0), EXPERTS),
    "clustered": (lambda ffn, E: clustered_plan(ffn.gate, ffn.up, E, seed=0), [8, 32]),
}


def study_layer(ffn: DenseFFN, x: torch.Tensor, router_steps: int) -> dict:
    """All plans / expert counts for one layer, on real activations x."""
    n = x.shape[0]
    n_train = n // 2
    x_train, x_test = x[:n_train], x[n_train:]
    out: dict = {"n_tokens": n, "n_train": n_train}

    # sanity: partitioning must be exact on these real activations
    p1 = PartitionedFFN(ffn, contiguous_plan(ffn.intermediate, 8))
    out["exactness_rel_error"] = p1.dense_equivalent_error(x_test)
    assert out["exactness_rel_error"] < 1e-4, out["exactness_rel_error"]

    for pname, (factory, es) in PLANS.items():
        for E in es:
            plan = factory(ffn, E)
            p = PartitionedFFN(ffn, plan)
            sizes = torch.tensor(plan.sizes(), dtype=torch.float)
            outs = p.expert_outputs(x_test)                    # (E, T, hidden)
            conc = contribution_concentration(outs)

            oracle = {}
            for k in TOPKS:
                if k <= E:
                    oracle[k] = oracle_topk_residual(outs, k)

            # cheap learnable router, trained on the other half of the tokens
            outs_train = p.expert_outputs(x_train)
            train_targets = outs_train.norm(dim=2).argmax(dim=0)   # oracle top-1
            wb = train_linear_router(x_train, train_targets, E, steps=router_steps)
            feats = torch.cat(
                [x_test, torch.ones(x_test.shape[0], 1, device=x_test.device)], dim=1
            )
            scores = feats @ wb
            test_targets = outs.norm(dim=2).argmax(dim=0)
            learned = {}
            for k in TOPKS:
                if k <= E:
                    learned[k] = router_topk_residual(outs, scores, k)

            out[f"{pname}_E{E}"] = {
                "expert_sizes": {
                    "min": int(sizes.min()), "max": int(sizes.max()),
                    "mean": float(sizes.mean()),
                    "max_over_mean": float(sizes.max() / sizes.mean()),
                    "largest_share": float(sizes.max() / sizes.sum()),
                },
                "concentration": conc,
                "oracle": oracle,
                "learned_router": learned,
                # how often the cheap router agrees with the best expert
                "router_top1_accuracy": float(
                    (scores.argmax(dim=1) == test_targets).float().mean()
                ),
            }
    return out


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="results/pilot_sparsity.json")
    ap.add_argument("--layers", type=int, nargs="*", default=TARGET_LAYERS)
    ap.add_argument("--router-steps", type=int, default=300)
    args = ap.parse_args()

    text = build_calibration_text()
    print(f"calibration corpus: {len(text):,} characters")

    caps, model = capture_activations(MODEL_ID, args.layers, text)
    print("captured MLP inputs for layers:",
          {k: tuple(v.shape) for k, v in caps.items()})

    report = {"model": MODEL_ID, "layers": {}, "calibration_chars": len(text)}
    for layer, x in sorted(caps.items()):
        print(f"\n=== layer {layer} · {x.shape[0]} tokens · hidden {x.shape[1]} ===",
              flush=True)
        ffn = ffn_from_module(model, layer)
        print(f"  FFN: intermediate={ffn.intermediate} "
              f"(expansion {ffn.intermediate/ffn.hidden:.2f})")
        res = study_layer(ffn, x, args.router_steps)
        report["layers"][str(layer)] = {"geometry": {
            "hidden": ffn.hidden, "intermediate": ffn.intermediate},
            **res}
        for key, v in res.items():
            if not isinstance(v, dict) or "oracle" not in v:
                continue
            best_k = min(v["oracle"], key=lambda k: v["oracle"][k]["mean_rel_residual"])
            print(f"    {key:16s} largest_share={v['expert_sizes']['largest_share']:.3f}"
                  f"  PR={v['concentration']['participation_ratio']:.1f}"
                  f"  oracle(k={best_k})={v['oracle'][best_k]['mean_rel_residual']:.4f}"
                  f"  learned(k={best_k})="
                  f"{v['learned_router'][best_k]['mean_rel_residual']:.4f}")

    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.out) or ".", exist_ok=True)
    with open(args.out, "w") as fh:
        json.dump(report, fh, indent=2)
    print(f"\nwrote {args.out}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())


===== experiments/quant_perplexity.py =====

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
quant_perplexity.py -- how much does a quantisation cost the model?

Why this exists
---------------
The repo's quality gate (experiments/eval_gate.py) is pass/fail on 19-30 tasks
with exact-match checks. On those tasks Q2_K and Q4_K_M of the same model score
the same, and the failures move inside the same handful of brittle constraints --
so the gate says "no measurable difference" and cannot say "Q2 is fine". It is
not the right instrument for the question a user actually asks ("will the small
quant make my model dumber?"), because the answer lives in the *distribution*,
not in whether an easy answer is right.

Perplexity is that instrument. Same weights, same text, same token stream, only
the quantisation differs; the difference in negative log-likelihood per token is
the damage. It is also the measurement that shows why the quant sizes in this
repo are what they are.

What it does
------------
1. builds one fixed corpus from this repo's own prose and code (the text the user
   of this repo is actually pointing the model at), and records its sha256;
2. runs `llama-perplexity` once per `--model LABEL=PATH`, identically
   (`-c/--chunks/--threads` held fixed), optionally under `tools/mem_watchdog.py`;
3. parses each run's final estimate and its per-chunk series, and writes a JSON
   report with the command, the corpus hash, the runtime, and whether a guard
   ended the run.

A detail that matters when reading the output: llama-perplexity prints a
*CUMULATIVE* perplexity after each chunk, not a per-chunk value -- the last
number equals the final estimate exactly. The per-chunk list is recorded here for
provenance only; do not difference it into per-chunk perplexities.

Usage
-----
    python3 experiments/quant_perplexity.py \\
        --model q2=$HOME/models/Ornith-1.5-35B-A3B-Q2_K.gguf \\
        --model q4=$HOME/models/Ornith-1.5-35B-A3B-Q4_K_M.gguf \\
        --out results/quant_perplexity.json

`--self-test` checks the parser and the corpus builder offline, with no model.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import glob
import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
import shutil
import struct
import subprocess
import sys
import time

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
REPO = os.path.dirname(HERE)

# The unsloth build is the one that runs on this host: the llama.cpp-upstream
# build ships a llama-perplexity that segfaults on any model here (SIGSEGV before
# it prints anything). Override with --perplexity.
DEFAULT_PERPLEXITY = os.path.expanduser("~/.unsloth/llama.cpp/llama-perplexity")
WATCHDOG = os.path.join(REPO, "tools", "mem_watchdog.py")

# Prose first, then code: the same ordering every run, so two runs see an
# identical token stream even if one model tokenises slightly differently.
CORPUS_SOURCES = ["README.md", "docs/*.md", "src/moeme/*.py", "experiments/*.py"]

FINAL_RE = re.compile(r"Final estimate: PPL = ([0-9.]+) \+/- ([0-9.]+)")
CHUNK_RE = re.compile(r"\[(\d+)\]([0-9.]+)")


def build_corpus(out_path: str, sources: list[str] | None = None) -> dict:
    """Concatenate fixed, sorted sources into one corpus file.

    Returns {path, sha256, bytes, files}. Sorted globs keep two runs -- and two
    architectures -- on the same tokens, which is the whole basis of the
    comparison.
    """
    sources = sources if sources is not None else CORPUS_SOURCES
    paths: list[str] = []
    for pattern in sources:
        full = os.path.join(REPO, pattern)
        if any(c in pattern for c in "*?["):
            paths.extend(sorted(glob.glob(full)))
        elif os.path.exists(full):
            paths.append(full)
    if not paths:
        raise SystemExit(f"no corpus sources found under {REPO} for {sources}")
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(out_path) or ".", exist_ok=True)
    with open(out_path, "wb") as out:
        for p in paths:
            out.write(f"\n\n===== {os.path.relpath(p, REPO)} =====\n\n".encode())
            with open(p, "rb") as fh:
                out.write(fh.read())
    data = open(out_path, "rb").read()
    return {"path": out_path, "sha256": hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest(),
            "bytes": len(data), "files": [os.path.relpath(p, REPO) for p in paths]}


def parse_ppl(text: str) -> dict:
    """Pull the final estimate and the printed series out of a perplexity log."""
    final = FINAL_RE.search(text)
    series = [float(v) for _, v in CHUNK_RE.findall(text)]
    return {
        "ppl": float(final.group(1)) if final else None,
        "stderr": float(final.group(2)) if final else None,
        # Cumulative, not per-chunk -- see the module docstring.
        "cumulative_series": series,
        "chunks_reported": len(series),
        "final_estimate_line": final.group(0) if final else None,
    }


def run_one(label: str, model: str, corpus: str, ctx: int, chunks: int,
            threads: int, perplexity: str, guard: bool, min_ram_gib: float,
            gpu_layers: int, cpu_moe: bool, log_dir: str,
            extra: list[str] | None = None) -> dict:
    """Run llama-perplexity once, optionally under the memory watchdog."""
    os.makedirs(log_dir, exist_ok=True)
    log = os.path.join(log_dir, f"ppl_{label}.log")
    argv = [perplexity, "-m", model, "-f", corpus, "-c", str(ctx),
            "--chunks", str(chunks), "-t", str(threads),
            "-ngl", str(gpu_layers)]
    if cpu_moe:
        argv.append("-cmoe")
    argv.extend(extra or [])
    cmd = " ".join(argv)
    t0 = time.perf_counter()
    with open(log, "w") as fh:
        proc = subprocess.Popen(argv, stdout=fh, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
    guard_out = ""
    guard_log = os.path.join(log_dir, f"ppl_{label}.watch.log")
    if guard:
        w = subprocess.run([sys.executable, WATCHDOG, "--pid", str(proc.pid),
                            "--min-ram-gib", str(min_ram_gib),
                            "--grace-samples", "2", "--interval", "3",
                            "--log", guard_log], capture_output=True, text=True)
        guard_out = (w.stdout or "").strip()
    rc = proc.wait()
    wall = time.perf_counter() - t0
    text = open(log, errors="replace").read()
    parsed = parse_ppl(text)
    parsed.update({"label": label, "model": model, "gguf_bytes": os.path.getsize(model),
                   "exit_code": rc, "wall_s": wall, "command": cmd,
                   "guard": guard_out or None,
                   "guard_ended_it": "GUARD TERMINATED" in guard_out})
    print(f"{label:<10} ppl={parsed['ppl']} exit={rc} wall={wall:.0f}s "
          f"{'-- ' + guard_out if guard_out else ''}", flush=True)
    return parsed


# GGUF metadata value types (llama.cpp gguf.h). Only metadata is read here; the
# tensor data is never touched.
_GGUF_SCALARS = {0: ("<B", 1), 1: ("<b", 1), 2: ("<H", 2), 3: ("<h", 2),
                 4: ("<I", 4), 5: ("<i", 4), 6: ("<f", 4), 7: ("<B", 1),
                 10: ("<Q", 8), 11: ("<q", 8), 12: ("<d", 8)}
_GGUF_STRING = 8
_GGUF_ARRAY = 9

# general.file_type, the GGUF file-type enum -- a property of the artifact, not a
# guess made from its filename.
FILE_TYPE_NAMES = {
    0: "F32", 1: "F16", 2: "Q4_0", 3: "Q4_1", 4: "Q4_1_SOME_F16", 5: "BF16",
    7: "Q8_0", 8: "Q5_0", 9: "Q5_1", 10: "Q2_K", 11: "Q3_K_S", 12: "Q3_K_M",
    13: "Q3_K_L", 14: "Q4_K_S", 15: "Q4_K_M", 16: "Q5_K_S", 17: "Q5_K_M",
    18: "Q6_K", 19: "IQ2_XXS", 20: "IQ2_XS", 21: "IQ2_S", 22: "IQ3_XS",
    23: "IQ3_XXS", 24: "IQ1_S", 25: "IQ4_NL", 26: "IQ3_S", 27: "IQ2_M",
}


def _read_gguf_value(fh, vtype: int, *, keep: bool = True):
    """Read one metadata value, or consume it without keeping it.

    Every value must be *consumed* even when it is not wanted: a reader that skips
    a key it does not recognise loses alignment and misreads every key after it.
    """
    if vtype in _GGUF_SCALARS:
        fmt, size = _GGUF_SCALARS[vtype]
        raw = fh.read(size)
        if len(raw) != size:
            raise ValueError("truncated GGUF metadata")
        return struct.unpack(fmt, raw)[0] if keep else None
    if vtype == _GGUF_STRING:
        n, = struct.unpack("<Q", fh.read(8))
        raw = fh.read(n)
        if len(raw) != n:
            raise ValueError("truncated GGUF string")
        return raw.decode("utf-8", "replace") if keep else None
    if vtype == _GGUF_ARRAY:
        elem, = struct.unpack("<I", fh.read(4))
        count, = struct.unpack("<Q", fh.read(8))
        if not keep:
            for _ in range(count):
                _read_gguf_value(fh, elem, keep=False)
            return None
        return [_read_gguf_value(fh, elem) for _ in range(count)]
    raise ValueError(f"unknown GGUF metadata type {vtype}")


def gguf_metadata(path: str, keys: set[str] | None = None) -> dict:
    """Read a GGUF's metadata (never its tensors), optionally only `keys`.

    GGUF v1/v2 store the two counts as uint32; v3 widened them to uint64.
    """
    meta: dict = {}
    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
        if fh.read(4) != b"GGUF":
            raise ValueError(f"{path}: not a GGUF file")
        version, = struct.unpack("<I", fh.read(4))
        wide = "<Q" if version >= 3 else "<I"
        struct.unpack(wide, fh.read(struct.calcsize(wide)))  # n_tensors
        n_kv, = struct.unpack(wide, fh.read(struct.calcsize(wide)))
        for _ in range(n_kv):
            n, = struct.unpack("<Q", fh.read(8))
            key = fh.read(n).decode("utf-8", "replace")
            vtype, = struct.unpack("<I", fh.read(4))
            want = keys is None or key in keys
            value = _read_gguf_value(fh, vtype, keep=want)
            if want:
                meta[key] = value
    return meta


def fmt_bpw(path: str) -> str:
    """Quantisation from the GGUF's own metadata (Q2_K / Q4_K_M / ...), or "?".

    "?" is a real answer, not a failure to try. A quantisation tool may omit
    general.file_type, and a name inferred from the *filename* would put an
    unverified string into a results file that is quoted as a measurement.

    Fixing this to read real artifacts mattered: the tokenizer's token array sits
    in front of general.file_type in these files, and the first version of this
    reader -- scalar types only, arrays skipped by their printed width -- lost
    alignment on it and reported "?" for both GGUFs, i.e. it silently failed to
    report the exact property the report exists to record.
    """
    try:
        value = gguf_metadata(path, keys={"general.file_type"}).get(
            "general.file_type")
    except (OSError, ValueError, struct.error):
        return "?"
    return FILE_TYPE_NAMES.get(value, "?") if isinstance(value, int) else "?"


def self_test() -> int:
    """Offline: the parser and the corpus builder, no model and no GPU."""
    sample = (
        "0.05.265.837 I perplexity: calculating perplexity over 2 chunks, n_ctx=512\n"
        "[1]8.3929,[2]6.5966,\n"
        "1.29.771.068 I perplexity: 74.63 seconds per pass - ETA 2.48 minutes\n"
        "2.46.871.431 I Final estimate: PPL = 6.5966 +/- 0.38006\n")
    got = parse_ppl(sample)
    assert got["ppl"] == 6.5966, got
    assert got["stderr"] == 0.38006, got
    assert got["cumulative_series"] == [8.3929, 6.5966], got
    assert got["chunks_reported"] == 2, got
    # A run the OOM killer ended has no final estimate; that must read as "no
    # number", never as a pass or a zero.
    killed = parse_ppl("0.00.930.529 W llama_model_loader: tensor overrides\n")
    assert killed["ppl"] is None and killed["cumulative_series"] == [], killed
    import tempfile
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        c = build_corpus(os.path.join(tmp, "c.txt"))
        assert c["bytes"] > 1000 and len(c["sha256"]) == 64, c
        again = build_corpus(os.path.join(tmp, "c2.txt"))
        assert again["sha256"] == c["sha256"], "corpus must be deterministic"
        # A real artifact states its quantisation *after* the tokenizer's token
        # array. A reader that cannot consume an array loses alignment there and
        # reports "?" for a file that does state it -- which is how the first
        # version of this knowingly failed. This is that regression.
        meta = os.path.join(tmp, "m.gguf")
        key1, key2 = b"tokenizer.ggml.tokens", b"general.file_type"
        with open(meta, "wb") as fh:
            fh.write(b"GGUF" + struct.pack("<I", 3) + struct.pack("<Q", 0))
            fh.write(struct.pack("<Q", 2))
            fh.write(struct.pack("<Q", len(key1)) + key1
                     + struct.pack("<I", _GGUF_ARRAY) + struct.pack("<I", _GGUF_STRING)
                     + struct.pack("<Q", 2000)
                     + b"".join(struct.pack("<Q", 2) + b"xy" for _ in range(2000)))
            fh.write(struct.pack("<Q", len(key2)) + key2
                     + struct.pack("<I", 4) + struct.pack("<I", 10))
        assert fmt_bpw(meta) == "Q2_K", fmt_bpw(meta)
    print("quant_perplexity self-test: ok")
    return 0


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--model", action="append", default=[], metavar="LABEL=PATH",
                    help="a GGUF to score; repeat for each quantisation")
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="results/quant_perplexity.json")
    ap.add_argument("--corpus", default="results/ppl_corpus.txt")
    ap.add_argument("--log-dir", default="logs")
    ap.add_argument("--ctx", type=int, default=512)
    ap.add_argument("--chunks", type=int, default=8)
    ap.add_argument("--threads", type=int, default=6)
    ap.add_argument("--gpu-layers", type=int, default=99,
                    help="layers to offload; 99 = all that fit")
    ap.add_argument("--cpu-moe", action="store_true",
                    help="keep every expert on the CPU. Fatal for a 21 GB MoE on "
                         "a 15 GiB host: loading it that way was OOM-killed here")
    ap.add_argument("--perplexity", default=DEFAULT_PERPLEXITY)
    ap.add_argument("--no-guard", action="store_true",
                    help="do not wrap each run in tools/mem_watchdog.py")
    ap.add_argument("--min-ram-gib", type=float, default=1.5)
    ap.add_argument("--self-test", action="store_true")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    if args.self_test:
        return self_test()
    if not args.model:
        raise SystemExit("give at least one --model LABEL=PATH")
    if not shutil.which(args.perplexity) and not os.path.exists(args.perplexity):
        raise SystemExit(f"no llama-perplexity at {args.perplexity}; "
                         f"pass --perplexity")

    corpus = build_corpus(args.corpus)
    print(f"corpus {corpus['path']}: {corpus['bytes']} bytes from "
          f"{len(corpus['files'])} files, sha256 {corpus['sha256'][:16]}...\n")

    report = {"corpus": corpus, "ctx": args.ctx, "chunks": args.chunks,
              "threads": args.threads, "gpu_layers": args.gpu_layers,
              "cpu_moe": args.cpu_moe,
              "perplexity_binary": args.perplexity, "runs": {}}
    for spec in args.model:
        if "=" not in spec:
            raise SystemExit(f"bad --model {spec!r}, want LABEL=PATH")
        label, path = spec.split("=", 1)
        run = run_one(label, path, corpus["path"], args.ctx, args.chunks,
                      args.threads, args.perplexity, not args.no_guard,
                      args.min_ram_gib, args.gpu_layers, args.cpu_moe,
                      args.log_dir)
        run["quant"] = fmt_bpw(path)
        report["runs"][label] = run
        save(report, args.out)

    # The comparison is only meaningful when two runs saw the same tokens.
    scored = {k: v["ppl"] for k, v in report["runs"].items() if v["ppl"]}
    if len(scored) >= 2:
        print()
        for k, v in scored.items():
            print(f"  {k:<10} PPL {v:.4f}")
        lo, hi = min(scored, key=scored.get), max(scored, key=scored.get)
        delta = scored[hi] - scored[lo]
        print(f"  {hi} is {delta:+.4f} PPL ({100 * delta / scored[lo]:+.1f}%) "
              f"against {lo}")
    missing = [k for k, v in report["runs"].items() if not v["ppl"]]
    if missing:
        print(f"\n  no estimate for {', '.join(missing)} -- read the recorded "
              f"exit code and guard verdict before comparing anything")
    print(f"wrote {args.out}")
    return 0


def save(report: dict, path: str) -> None:
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path) or ".", exist_ok=True)
    tmp = path + ".tmp"
    with open(tmp, "w") as fh:
        json.dump(report, fh, indent=2)
    os.replace(tmp, path)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())


===== experiments/upcycle_poc.py =====

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
upcycle_poc.py -- does *training* beat the frozen ceiling?

The question
------------
`experiments/pilot_sparsity.py` measured the no-retrain ceiling: slicing a
trained dense FFN into E experts and keeping k of them loses most of the layer
output (Qwen3.5-0.8B layer 0, E=8, k=1: mean relative residual 0.746). That
experiment touched no weights, so it says nothing about gradient descent.

This driver upcycles the FFN with `moeme.upcycle.MoEFFN` and *trains* the
experts and router, then measures three things:

  1. `val_ppl` dense vs untrained-MoE vs trained-MoE. The untrained number is
     the post-hoc path; the trained number is the upcycling path.
  2. `residual` = how well the MoE reproduces the *original* dense layer output
     on held-out activations. Same metric as the pilot's oracle, so the trained
     number can be compared to the frozen ceiling directly.
     **If trained residual < frozen-oracle residual, training created structure
     that a frozen partition did not have.**
  3. routing diagnostics: does the router specialise (effective experts) or
     collapse (all mass on one expert)?

Design notes
------------
* Only the upcycled layers' parameters are trained; the rest of the model is
  frozen. That keeps the run affordable (0.8B fully trainable would still fit a
  T4, but this makes the experiment cheap and the effect attributable).
* `--self-test` exercises the whole pipeline end to end on a tiny random model
  with no download, so the training/eval/residual plumbing is verifiable
  offline. Run that before spending a Kaggle session.
* Checkpoints are written after every configuration, so a 12-hour session cap
  cannot lose a finished config.
* `--max-seconds` caps the *training* time of each configuration. On a rented
  device whose speed is unknown ahead of time (a free Kaggle T4, say) the
  honest failure mode is an unbounded loop that hits the session cap with
  nothing written. With a budget the step is stopped early and the report is
  still written, so a slow device returns fewer steps of evidence rather than
  no evidence. `steps_completed` and `stopped_early` record what happened.
* A configuration that raises is recorded in the report as an `error` entry and
  the remaining configurations still run; the process exits non-zero at the
  end, so the failure is not swallowed.

Usage
-----
    python3 experiments/upcycle_poc.py --self-test
    python3 experiments/upcycle_poc.py --experts 8 --topks 1 2 4 \
        --steps 400 --layers all --out results/upcycle_poc.json
    # an unattended cloud run: never exceed ~4 h of training per config
    python3 experiments/upcycle_poc.py --experts 64 --topks 1 --layers all \
        --max-seconds 14400 --out results/upcycle_poc.json
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import math
import os
import sys
import time
import traceback
from types import SimpleNamespace

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "src"))

import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F

from moeme.upcycle import (
    MoEConfig,
    collect_total_aux_loss,
    reconstruction_residual,
    upcycle_model,
)

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
PILOT_JSON = os.path.join(HERE, "..", "results", "pilot_sparsity.json")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# device / dtype
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def pick_amp_dtype(device: torch.device):
    if device.type != "cuda":
        return None
    if torch.cuda.is_bf16_supported():
        return torch.bfloat16
    return torch.float16


def make_grad_scaler(amp_dtype):
    enabled = amp_dtype == torch.float16
    try:
        return torch.amp.GradScaler("cuda", enabled=enabled)   # torch >= 2.4
    except (AttributeError, TypeError):
        return torch.cuda.amp.GradScaler(enabled=enabled)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# corpus
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

BUILTIN_TEXT = """\
A mixture of experts layer replaces one feed forward network with several and a
router that selects a small subset per token. Training such a layer from scratch
is expensive, so upcycling initialises the experts from an existing dense model
and continues pretraining, letting the router and the experts specialise.
The load balancing auxiliary loss discourages the router from collapsing onto a
few experts. A collapsed router wastes capacity: most experts receive no
gradient and the effective parameter count falls.
Inference cost is set by the active parameters, one expert per token, while the
storage cost is set by the total parameters, every expert on disk. Those two
numbers diverge exactly in proportion to the number of experts.
A laptop with eight gigabytes of video memory cannot hold a twenty seven billion
parameter model, so decoding streams weights from a solid state drive and the
achievable rate is set by storage throughput rather than by arithmetic.
""" * 40


def load_token_stream(args, tokenizer) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, str]:
    """A flat token stream, plus a description of where it came from.

    The description is returned rather than only printed because it lands in the
    report: a run that silently fell back to the built-in corpus still produces
    perfectly finite perplexities, and on a repeated 1 kB text those numbers
    mean nothing. An unattended run must be able to record which corpus it used.
    """
    text = None
    source = None
    if args.text_file:
        with open(args.text_file) as fh:
            text = fh.read()
        source = f"text-file:{args.text_file}"
        print(f"corpus: {args.text_file} ({len(text):,} chars)")
    elif not args.offline:
        try:
            from datasets import load_dataset
            ds = load_dataset(args.dataset, split="train", streaming=True)
            want = args.corpus_chars
            chunks, have = [], 0
            for row in ds:
                t = row.get("text")
                if not t:
                    continue
                chunks.append(t)
                have += len(t)
                if have >= want:
                    break
            text = "\n".join(chunks)
            source = f"dataset:{args.dataset}"
            print(f"corpus: {args.dataset} streaming, {have:,} chars "
                  f"from {len(chunks)} documents")
        except Exception as e:                                  # noqa: BLE001
            print(f"corpus: dataset load failed ({type(e).__name__}: {e}); "
                  f"falling back to the built-in corpus")
    if text is None:
        text = BUILTIN_TEXT
        source = "built-in"
        print(f"corpus: built-in ({len(text):,} chars)")

    ids = tokenizer(text, return_tensors="pt").input_ids[0]
    print(f"corpus: {ids.numel():,} tokens")
    return ids, source


def make_blocks(ids: torch.Tensor, seq_len: int, limit: int | None = None):
    """Non-overlapping (input_ids, labels) blocks.

    `labels` is deliberately the **same tensor** as `input_ids`: Hugging Face
    causal LM heads shift internally (`logits[..., :-1]` against
    `labels[..., 1:]`). Pre-shifting here shifts twice, giving a misaligned,
    near-random loss -- which is exactly what happened, and it read as a dense
    perplexity of 20,664 where the true figure is ~13. If this ever looks wrong
    again, check this first.
    """
    n = ids.numel() // seq_len
    if limit:
        n = min(n, limit)
    blocks = []
    for i in range(n):
        chunk = ids[i * seq_len:(i + 1) * seq_len]
        if chunk.numel() < seq_len:
            break
        blocks.append((chunk.unsqueeze(0), chunk.unsqueeze(0)))
    return blocks


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# evaluation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@torch.no_grad()
def eval_perplexity(model, blocks, device, amp_dtype, max_blocks=8) -> float:
    model.eval()
    total, ntok = 0.0, 0
    for x, y in blocks[:max_blocks]:
        x, y = x.to(device), y.to(device)
        if amp_dtype is not None:
            with torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=amp_dtype):
                out = model(x, labels=y)
        else:
            out = model(x, labels=y)
        total += out.loss.item() * y.numel()
        ntok += y.numel()
    return math.exp(total / max(ntok, 1))


@torch.no_grad()
def capture_reference(model, batches, layers, device) -> dict:
    """Per-layer (mlp input, dense mlp output) on a fixed held-out batch.

    Captured *before* upcycling, so it is the original dense function and stays
    a valid reference after training has moved the MoE weights.
    """
    layer_mods = model.model.layers
    caps: dict[int, dict] = {i: {} for i in layers}

    def pre(i):
        def fn(_m, args):
            caps[i]["x"] = args[0].detach().reshape(-1, args[0].shape[-1]).float()
        return fn

    def post(i):
        def fn(_m, _args, out):
            caps[i]["y"] = out.detach().reshape(-1, out.shape[-1]).float()
        return fn

    handles = []
    for i in layers:
        handles.append(layer_mods[i].mlp.register_forward_pre_hook(pre(i)))
        handles.append(layer_mods[i].mlp.register_forward_hook(post(i)))

    model.eval()
    x = batches[0][0].to(device)
    model(x)
    for h in handles:
        h.remove()
    return {i: {k: v.cpu() for k, v in d.items() if v is not None}
            for i, d in caps.items() if "x" in d and "y" in d}


@torch.no_grad()
def evaluate_layers(moes, reference, device, dtype, top_k: int) -> dict:
    """Per-layer sparsity/preservation metrics for the *current* weights.

    * `vs_original_dense` -- how well the sparse layer still reproduces the
      pretrained dense function it replaced. This one **can exceed 1.0**: once
      training rescales experts to compensate for the experts that are never
      selected, the output can end up farther from the original than predicting
      zero would be. That is a real property of the trained model, not a bug.
      ("1.0 = no better than predicting zero" is the same scale the pilot used.)
    * `oracle_topk` -- best-case top-k against the module's **own** all-experts
      sum. This is exactly what the pilot measured, but on the *trained*
      weights, which is what makes trained-vs-frozen a fair comparison.
    * `router_topk` -- what the learned router actually achieves against that
      same all-experts sum. The oracle gap bounds what a better router could buy.
    """
    from moeme.ffn import oracle_topk_residual, router_topk_residual

    out: dict[int, dict] = {}
    by_layer = {i: m for i, m in moes}
    for i, ref in reference.items():
        m = by_layer.get(i)
        if m is None:
            continue
        x = ref["x"].to(device=device, dtype=dtype)
        outs = m.expert_outputs(x)                        # (E, T, hidden)
        scores = m.router_scores(x)                       # (T, E)
        k = min(top_k, m.n_experts)
        out[i] = {
            "vs_original_dense": reconstruction_residual(ref["y"].to(device), m(x)),
            "oracle_topk": oracle_topk_residual(outs, k)["mean_rel_residual"],
            "router_topk": router_topk_residual(outs, scores, k)["mean_rel_residual"],
        }
    if device.type == "cuda":
        torch.cuda.empty_cache()
    return out


def summarize_layers(metrics: dict) -> dict:
    def mean(field):
        vals = [m[field] for m in metrics.values()]
        return sum(vals) / len(vals) if vals else float("nan")
    return {
        "vs_original_dense_mean": mean("vs_original_dense"),
        "oracle_topk_mean": mean("oracle_topk"),
        "router_topk_mean": mean("router_topk"),
    }


def routing_summary(moes) -> dict:
    per_layer, effs, loads, ents = {}, [], [], []
    for i, m in moes:
        s = m.last_stats
        per_layer[i] = s
        effs.append(s.get("effective_experts", float("nan")))
        loads.append(s.get("load_max_over_mean", float("nan")))
        ents.append(s.get("usage_entropy_normalised", float("nan")))

    def mean(v):
        vals = [x for x in v if x == x]                     # drop NaNs
        return sum(vals) / len(vals) if vals else float("nan")

    return {
        "per_layer": per_layer,
        "effective_experts_mean": mean(effs),
        "load_max_over_mean": mean(loads),
        "usage_entropy_mean": mean(ents),
    }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# training
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def load_pilot_oracle(experts: int, topk: int, layers) -> dict | None:
    """The pilot's frozen-oracle number for the same (E, k), if recorded."""
    try:
        with open(PILOT_JSON) as fh:
            pilot = json.load(fh)
    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
        return None
    out = {}
    for layer_str, data in pilot.get("layers", {}).items():
        if data.get("geometry", {}).get("intermediate") is None:
            continue
        key = f"random_E{experts}"
        block = data.get(key) or data.get(f"contiguous_E{experts}")
        if not block:
            continue
        oracle = block.get("oracle", {}).get(str(topk))
        if oracle:
            out[int(layer_str)] = {
                "mean_rel_residual": oracle["mean_rel_residual"],
                "plan_key": key,
            }
    return {"model": pilot.get("model"), "per_layer": out} if out else None


def train_one_config(*, model, originals, blocks_mod, config,
                     args, train_blocks, val_blocks, reference, device,
                     amp_dtype, scaler, dense_ppl: float) -> dict:
    """Upcycle, measure untrained, train, measure trained. One (E, k)."""
    layers = list(range(len(blocks_mod))) if args.layers == ["all"] \
        else [int(v) for v in args.layers]

    # restore the original dense MLPs, then upcycle afresh (identical init)
    for i, orig in originals:
        setattr(blocks_mod[i], "mlp", orig)
    for m in model.modules():
        for p in m.parameters(recurse=False):
            p.requires_grad_(False)

    t0 = time.perf_counter()
    moes = upcycle_model(model, config, layers)
    trainable = [p for p in model.parameters() if p.requires_grad]
    n_train = sum(p.numel() for p in trainable)
    print(f"  upcycled {len(moes)} layers, trainable params {n_train/1e6:.1f}M")

    # --- untrained (the post-hoc path) ---
    untrained_ppl = eval_perplexity(model, val_blocks, device, amp_dtype,
                                    args.eval_blocks)
    untrained_m = evaluate_layers(moes, reference, device,
                                  next(model.parameters()).dtype, config.top_k)

    # --- dense-equivalence integration check ---
    saved_k = [(m, m.config) for _, m in moes]
    for _, m in moes:
        m.config = m.config.with_(top_k=m.n_experts)
    dense_limit_ppl = eval_perplexity(model, val_blocks, device, amp_dtype,
                                      args.eval_blocks)
    for m, cfg in saved_k:
        m.config = cfg

    # --- train ---
    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(trainable, lr=args.lr, weight_decay=args.weight_decay,
                            betas=(0.9, 0.95))
    gen = torch.Generator().manual_seed(args.seed)
    ckpt_path = os.path.join(args.ckpt_dir, f"{config_tag(config, args)}.pt")
    start_step = 0
    if args.resume and ckpt_path and os.path.exists(ckpt_path):
        state = torch.load(ckpt_path, map_location=device, weights_only=False)
        for (i, m) in moes:
            m.load_state_dict(state["moes"][i])
        opt.load_state_dict(state["opt"])
        start_step = state["step"]
        print(f"  resumed from {ckpt_path} at step {start_step}")

    model.train()
    curve = []
    t_train = time.perf_counter()
    accum = max(1, args.grad_accum)
    budget = float(getattr(args, "max_seconds", 0) or 0)
    stopped_early = False
    steps_done = start_step
    for step in range(start_step, args.steps):
        if budget and (time.perf_counter() - t_train) > budget:
            print(f"  wall-clock budget {budget:.0f}s reached before step {step}; "
                  f"stopping training and reporting what was measured",
                  flush=True)
            stopped_early = True
            break
        opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
        total_loss = 0.0
        for _ in range(accum):
            k = int(torch.randint(len(train_blocks), (1,), generator=gen).item())
            x, y = train_blocks[k]
            x, y = x.to(device), y.to(device)
            if amp_dtype is not None:
                with torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=amp_dtype):
                    out = model(x, labels=y)
                    aux = collect_total_aux_loss(model)
                    loss = (out.loss + aux) / accum
                scaler.scale(loss).backward()
            else:
                out = model(x, labels=y)
                aux = collect_total_aux_loss(model)
                loss = (out.loss + aux) / accum
                loss.backward()
            total_loss += float(loss.item())
        if scaler.is_enabled():
            scaler.unscale_(opt)
            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(trainable, args.clip)
            scaler.step(opt)
            scaler.update()
        else:
            torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(trainable, args.clip)
            opt.step()
        # progress at most every 25 steps, and always report elapsed time so the
        # per-step cost is visible immediately. That matters: cost scales with
        # (upcycled layers x experts), so a subset at E=8 and all layers at E=64
        # differ by ~48x, which is the difference between minutes and a session.
        every = max(1, min(args.steps // 10, 25))
        if step % every == 0 or step == args.steps - 1:
            elapsed = time.perf_counter() - t_train
            # `elapsed` is stored, not merely printed: it is the only way to
            # recover the measured step cost from the report afterwards, which
            # is what sizing a later run on a different device depends on.
            curve.append({"step": step, "loss": total_loss, "elapsed": elapsed})
            print(f"    step {step:5d}  loss {total_loss:.4f}  "
                  f"elapsed {elapsed:6.0f}s", flush=True)
        if args.save_every and (step + 1) % args.save_every == 0:
            _save(ckpt_path, moes, opt, step + 1)
        steps_done = step + 1

    trained_ppl = eval_perplexity(model, val_blocks, device, amp_dtype,
                                  args.eval_blocks)
    # evaluate_layers runs every MoE layer forward, which also refreshes the
    # per-layer routing statistics used below
    trained_m = evaluate_layers(moes, reference, device,
                                next(model.parameters()).dtype, config.top_k)
    stats = routing_summary(moes)

    def mean(vals):
        vals = list(vals)
        return sum(vals) / len(vals) if vals else float("nan")

    result = {
        "config": {
            "n_experts": config.n_experts, "top_k": config.top_k,
            "combine": config.combine, "plan": config.plan,
            "aux_loss_coef": config.aux_loss_coef, "router_hidden": config.router_hidden,
            "expert_noise": config.expert_noise,
        },
        "layers": layers,
        "trainable_params": n_train,
        "steps": args.steps,
        "steps_completed": steps_done,
        "stopped_early": stopped_early,
        "max_seconds": budget,
        "dense_val_ppl": dense_ppl,
        "dense_limit_val_ppl": dense_limit_ppl,
        "untrained_val_ppl": untrained_ppl,
        "trained_val_ppl": trained_ppl,
        "metrics_untrained": summarize_layers(untrained_m),
        "metrics_trained": summarize_layers(trained_m),
        "metrics_per_layer": {
            "untrained": {str(k): v for k, v in untrained_m.items()},
            "trained": {str(k): v for k, v in trained_m.items()},
        },
        "routing": stats,
        "loss_curve": curve,
        "wall_seconds": time.perf_counter() - t0,
    }
    frozen = load_pilot_oracle(config.n_experts, config.top_k, layers)
    if frozen:
        result["frozen_oracle_reference"] = frozen
        common = set(frozen["per_layer"]) & set(trained_m)
        if common:
            f_mean = mean(frozen["per_layer"][i]["mean_rel_residual"] for i in common)
            o_mean = mean(trained_m[i]["oracle_topk"] for i in common)
            r_mean = mean(trained_m[i]["router_topk"] for i in common)
            result["beats_frozen_oracle"] = bool(o_mean < f_mean)
            result["frozen_oracle_mean_on_common_layers"] = f_mean
            result["trained_oracle_mean_on_common_layers"] = o_mean
            result["trained_router_mean_on_common_layers"] = r_mean
    return result


def config_tag(config, args) -> str:
    return (f"E{config.n_experts}_k{config.top_k}_{config.combine}_"
            f"{config.plan}_s{args.seed}_{'-'.join(args.layers)}")


def _save(path, moes, opt, step):
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path) or ".", exist_ok=True)
    torch.save({
        "moes": {i: m.state_dict() for i, m in moes},
        "opt": opt.state_dict(),
        "step": step,
    }, path)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# self-test: the whole pipeline on a tiny random model, no download
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class _TinyMLP(torch.nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, h, i):
        super().__init__()
        self.gate_proj = torch.nn.Linear(h, i, bias=False)
        self.up_proj = torch.nn.Linear(h, i, bias=False)
        self.down_proj = torch.nn.Linear(i, h, bias=False)

    def forward(self, x):
        return self.down_proj(F.silu(self.gate_proj(x)) * self.up_proj(x))


class _TinyBlock(torch.nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, h, i):
        super().__init__()
        self.mlp = _TinyMLP(h, i)

    def forward(self, x):
        return x + self.mlp(x)


class _TinyLM(torch.nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, vocab=256, h=64, i=128, n=3):
        super().__init__()
        self.embed = torch.nn.Embedding(vocab, h)
        self.model = torch.nn.Module()
        self.model.layers = torch.nn.ModuleList([_TinyBlock(h, i) for _ in range(n)])
        self.head = torch.nn.Linear(h, vocab, bias=False)

    def forward(self, ids, labels=None):
        x = self.embed(ids)
        for b in self.model.layers:
            x = b(x)
        logits = self.head(x)
        loss = None
        if labels is not None:
            loss = F.cross_entropy(logits.reshape(-1, logits.shape[-1]),
                                   labels.reshape(-1))
        return SimpleNamespace(loss=loss, logits=logits)


def self_test() -> int:
    """Run upcycle -> untrained eval -> train -> residual on a tiny model."""
    torch.manual_seed(0)
    device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
    model = _TinyLM().to(device)
    # run the self-test in the same dtype the real run uses, so dtype bugs in the
    # forward path surface here rather than on a rented GPU (this one did)
    if device.type == "cuda" and torch.cuda.is_bf16_supported():
        model = model.to(torch.bfloat16)
    blocks_mod = model.model.layers
    originals = [(i, blocks_mod[i].mlp) for i in range(len(blocks_mod))]

    g = torch.Generator().manual_seed(1)
    ids = torch.randint(0, 256, (4096,), generator=g)
    blocks = make_blocks(ids, seq_len=128)
    train_blocks, val_blocks = blocks[:-2], blocks[-2:]

    model.eval()
    dense_ppl = eval_perplexity(model, val_blocks, device, None, max_blocks=2)
    reference = capture_reference(model, val_blocks, [0, 1, 2], device)
    print(f"dense ppl {dense_ppl:.3f}; reference layers {sorted(reference)}")

    config = MoEConfig(n_experts=4, top_k=2, plan="random", aux_loss_coef=0.01)
    args = SimpleNamespace(layers=["all"], lr=3e-3, weight_decay=0.0,
                           grad_accum=2, steps=40, clip=1.0, seed=0, resume=False,
                           max_seconds=0.0,
                           ckpt_dir=os.path.join(HERE, "..", ".cache", "moeme",
                                                 "selftest_ckpt"),
                           eval_blocks=2, save_every=0)
    scaler = make_grad_scaler(None)
    res = train_one_config(model=model, originals=originals, blocks_mod=blocks_mod,
                           config=config, args=args,
                           train_blocks=train_blocks, val_blocks=val_blocks,
                           reference=reference, device=device, amp_dtype=None,
                           scaler=scaler, dense_ppl=dense_ppl)
    print(json.dumps({k: v for k, v in res.items()
                      if k not in ("routing", "loss_curve", "metrics_per_layer",
                                   "config", "layers")}, indent=2))
    print("routing:", json.dumps(res["routing"]["per_layer"][0], indent=2))
    tr = res["metrics_trained"]
    assert res["dense_limit_val_ppl"] < dense_ppl * 1.5, res["dense_limit_val_ppl"]
    assert math.isfinite(res["trained_val_ppl"])
    assert math.isfinite(tr["oracle_topk_mean"]) and tr["oracle_topk_mean"] >= 0
    # the oracle is a ceiling: a learned router cannot beat the best-possible k
    assert tr["oracle_topk_mean"] <= tr["router_topk_mean"] + 1e-5, tr
    print("\nself-test OK")
    return 0


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# main
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def build_args(argv=None):
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__,
                                 formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument("--model", default=os.environ.get("MOEME_POC_MODEL",
                                                      "Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B"))
    ap.add_argument("--experts", type=int, nargs="+", default=[8])
    ap.add_argument("--topks", type=int, nargs="+", default=[1, 2, 4])
    ap.add_argument("--combine", default="sum", choices=["sum", "mean", "softmax"])
    ap.add_argument("--plan", default="random",
                    choices=["contiguous", "interleaved", "random", "clustered"])
    ap.add_argument("--layers", nargs="+", default=["all"],
                    help='layer indices, or the single token "all"')
    ap.add_argument("--steps", type=int, default=300)
    ap.add_argument("--lr", type=float, default=1e-4)
    ap.add_argument("--weight-decay", type=float, default=0.0)
    ap.add_argument("--aux-loss-coef", type=float, default=1e-2)
    ap.add_argument("--expert-noise", type=float, default=0.0)
    ap.add_argument("--router-hidden", type=int, default=None)
    ap.add_argument("--seq-len", type=int, default=512)
    ap.add_argument("--grad-accum", type=int, default=4)
    ap.add_argument("--clip", type=float, default=1.0)
    ap.add_argument("--eval-blocks", type=int, default=4)
    ap.add_argument("--corpus-chars", type=int, default=4_000_000)
    ap.add_argument("--dataset", default="HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu")
    ap.add_argument("--text-file", default=None)
    ap.add_argument("--offline", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=0)
    ap.add_argument("--save-every", type=int, default=50)
    ap.add_argument("--max-seconds", type=float, default=0.0,
                    help="wall-clock budget for each configuration's training "
                         "loop; 0 = unlimited. Set this on an unattended or "
                         "rented device: without it a run slower than expected "
                         "is killed by the session cap having written nothing.")
    ap.add_argument("--resume", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--ckpt-dir", default=os.path.join(".cache", "moeme", "poc_ckpt"))
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="results/upcycle_poc.json")
    ap.add_argument("--self-test", action="store_true")
    return ap.parse_args(argv)


def main() -> int:
    args = build_args()
    if args.self_test:
        return self_test()

    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer

    device = torch.device("cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu")
    if device.type != "cuda":
        print("WARNING: no CUDA device. Qwen3.5's Gated DeltaNet path needs "
              "causal_conv1d, which is CUDA-only in this environment.")
    amp_dtype = pick_amp_dtype(device)
    scaler = make_grad_scaler(amp_dtype)
    torch.manual_seed(args.seed)
    print(f"device={device} amp={amp_dtype} model={args.model}")

    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model)
    dtype = amp_dtype or torch.float32
    last = None
    for kw in ({"dtype": dtype}, {"torch_dtype": dtype}):
        try:
            model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
                args.model, low_cpu_mem_usage=True, **kw)
            break
        except TypeError as e:
            last = e
    else:
        raise last
    model = model.to(device)
    print(f"params {sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters())/1e9:.3f}B")

    ids, corpus_source = load_token_stream(args, tok)
    if corpus_source == "built-in" and not args.offline:
        print("  WARNING: the requested dataset did not load and the built-in "
              "corpus was used instead. Perplexities from a repeated 1 kB text "
              "are not comparable to a real corpus -- check network access "
              "before reading any ppl number below.")
    blocks = make_blocks(ids, args.seq_len)
    if len(blocks) < 4:
        raise SystemExit("corpus too small for train/val split; raise --corpus-chars")
    n_val = max(1, len(blocks) // 20)
    train_blocks, val_blocks = blocks[:-n_val], blocks[-n_val:]
    print(f"blocks: {len(train_blocks)} train, {len(val_blocks)} val")

    blocks_mod = model.model.layers
    originals = [(i, blocks_mod[i].mlp) for i in range(len(blocks_mod))]
    layers = list(range(len(blocks_mod))) if args.layers == ["all"] \
        else [int(v) for v in args.layers]

    model.eval()
    dense_ppl = eval_perplexity(model, val_blocks, device, amp_dtype,
                                args.eval_blocks)
    print(f"dense val ppl {dense_ppl:.4f}")
    if dense_ppl > 200:
        print("  WARNING: dense perplexity is implausibly high. For a pretrained "
              "model on natural text expect ~5-40. Usual cause: label "
              "misalignment in make_blocks, or the wrong corpus/tokenizer.")
    reference = capture_reference(model, val_blocks, layers, device)

    report = {
        "model": args.model,
        "device": str(device),
        "amp_dtype": str(amp_dtype),
        "corpus": corpus_source,
        "corpus_tokens": int(ids.numel()),
        "dense_val_ppl": dense_ppl,
        "seq_len": args.seq_len,
        "train_blocks": len(train_blocks),
        "val_blocks": len(val_blocks),
        "runs": [],
    }
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.out) or ".", exist_ok=True)

    failed = False
    for E in args.experts:
        for k in args.topks:
            if k > E:
                continue
            config = MoEConfig(
                n_experts=E, top_k=k, combine=args.combine, plan=args.plan,
                aux_loss_coef=args.aux_loss_coef, router_hidden=args.router_hidden,
                seed=args.seed, expert_noise=args.expert_noise,
            )
            print(f"\n=== E={E} k={k} combine={args.combine} plan={args.plan} ===",
                  flush=True)
            try:
                res = train_one_config(
                    model=model, originals=originals, blocks_mod=blocks_mod,
                    config=config, args=args,
                    train_blocks=train_blocks, val_blocks=val_blocks,
                    reference=reference, device=device, amp_dtype=amp_dtype,
                    scaler=scaler, dense_ppl=dense_ppl,
                )
            except Exception as e:                              # noqa: BLE001
                # record it, keep the other configurations runnable, and exit
                # non-zero at the end -- the failure stays visible, but a run
                # that already produced results is not thrown away with it
                failed = True
                traceback.print_exc()
                report["runs"].append({
                    "config": {"n_experts": E, "top_k": k,
                               "combine": args.combine, "plan": args.plan},
                    "layers": layers,
                    "error": f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}",
                    "traceback": traceback.format_exc(),
                })
                with open(args.out, "w") as fh:
                    json.dump(report, fh, indent=2)
                print(f"  FAILED: {type(e).__name__}: {e} -- recorded in "
                      f"{args.out}", flush=True)
                continue
            report["runs"].append(res)
            tag = "  [EARLY-STOPPED]" if res["stopped_early"] else ""
            print(f"  steps: {res['steps_completed']}/{res['steps']}{tag}")
            print(f"  ppl: dense {res['dense_val_ppl']:.4f} | "
                  f"untrained {res['untrained_val_ppl']:.4f} | "
                  f"trained {res['trained_val_ppl']:.4f} | "
                  f"dense-limit {res['dense_limit_val_ppl']:.4f}")
            ut, tr = res["metrics_untrained"], res["metrics_trained"]
            print(f"  top-k vs all-experts: untrained-oracle "
                  f"{ut['oracle_topk_mean']:.4f} -> trained-oracle "
                  f"{tr['oracle_topk_mean']:.4f} | trained-router "
                  f"{tr['router_topk_mean']:.4f} | effective_experts "
                  f"{res['routing']['effective_experts_mean']:.2f}")
            if "beats_frozen_oracle" in res:
                print(f"  frozen pilot oracle "
                      f"{res['frozen_oracle_mean_on_common_layers']:.4f} vs trained "
                      f"{res['trained_oracle_mean_on_common_layers']:.4f} "
                      f"-> beats_frozen_oracle={res['beats_frozen_oracle']}")
            with open(args.out, "w") as fh:          # write after every config
                json.dump(report, fh, indent=2)
            print(f"  wrote {args.out}")

    if failed:
        print("\ndone WITH FAILURES -- see the \"error\" entries in the report")
        return 1
    print("\ndone")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())


===== experiments/verify_real_ffn.py =====

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
verify_real_ffn.py -- verify dense-equivalent FFN partitioning on the REAL
Qwen3.8-27B weights (not synthetic tensors).

Why only one layer: the checkpoint is 55.6 GB, but every one of the 64 FFN
blocks has identical geometry (267,386,880 params, verified from the safetensors
headers). One block is therefore sufficient to verify the maths on real weights,
and it costs ~535 MB of ranged download instead of 55.6 GB.

What this establishes
---------------------
  1. EXACTNESS  -- the partitioned layer with all experts active reproduces the
     real dense layer to floating-point tolerance, for every plan and expert
     count, on real data.
  2. THROUGHPUT -- partitioning adds no meaningful cost to the all-active path.
  3. STRUCTURE  -- how concentrated the per-expert contributions are, i.e.
     whether a trained dense FFN has any natural structure a top-k router could
     exploit. This is the quantity that decides whether post-hoc FFN sparsifi-
     cation is viable at all.

What this does NOT establish
----------------------------
It does not measure end-to-end model quality. That needs real activations from
the full model (see docs/feasibility_report.md, "Limitations"). Inputs here are
synthetic distributions; the oracle numbers are therefore a structure probe, not
a quality prediction.

Usage:
    python3 experiments/verify_real_ffn.py --out results/real_ffn.json
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import os
import sys
import time

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "src"))
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "tools"))

import torch

from moeme.ffn import (
    DenseFFN,
    PartitionedFFN,
    clustered_plan,
    contiguous_plan,
    contribution_concentration,
    interleaved_plan,
    oracle_topk_residual,
    random_plan,
)
from hf_tensor import get_tensor, index_for  # noqa: E402

REPO = "Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B"
LAYER = 1  # a Gated DeltaNet layer; FFN geometry is identical in all 64

def tensor_names(layer: int) -> list[str]:
    p = f"model.language_model.layers.{layer}.mlp"
    return [f"{p}.gate_proj.weight", f"{p}.up_proj.weight", f"{p}.down_proj.weight"]


def load_real_ffn(layer: int = LAYER) -> DenseFFN:
    idx = index_for(REPO)
    names = tensor_names(layer)
    print(f"fetching {len(names)} tensors for layer {layer} (range requests, cached)...")
    t0 = time.perf_counter()
    tensors = [get_tensor(REPO, n, idx)[0] for n in names]
    print(f"  fetched in {time.perf_counter() - t0:.1f}s  "
          f"({sum(t.numel() for t in tensors) * 2 / 1e9:.3f} GB bf16)")
    gate, up, down = tensors
    return DenseFFN(gate=gate.float(), up=up.float(), down=down.float())


def make_inputs(hidden: int, n: int, kind: str, seed: int = 0) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Synthetic inputs. 'student_t' is heavy-tailed, closer to real LLM
    activation statistics than a Gaussian, but it is NOT a real activation."""
    g = torch.Generator().manual_seed(seed)
    if kind == "gaussian":
        return torch.randn(n, hidden, generator=g)
    if kind == "student_t":
        # df=3 gives realistic outlier kurtosis; scale to unit variance
        return (torch.randn(n, hidden, generator=g)
                / torch.randn(n, hidden, generator=g).pow(2).sqrt().clamp_min(1e-6)
                / (3 ** 0.5))
    raise ValueError(kind)


def weight_structure(ffn: DenseFFN) -> dict:
    """Basic statistics that show whether the intermediate axis is homogeneous."""
    gnorm = ffn.gate.norm(dim=1)
    unorm = ffn.up.norm(dim=1)
    feats = torch.cat([ffn.gate, ffn.up], dim=1)
    # per-axis mean/median/std give a cheap view of heterogeneity
    def stats(t: torch.Tensor) -> dict:
        return {
            "mean": float(t.mean()), "std": float(t.std()),
            "min": float(t.min()), "max": float(t.max()),
            "cv": float(t.std() / t.mean().clamp_min(1e-12)),
        }
    gn = stats(gnorm)
    un = stats(unorm)
    return {
        "intermediate": ffn.intermediate,
        "hidden": ffn.hidden,
        "gate_row_norm": gn,
        "up_row_norm": un,
        "gate_row_norm_cv": gn["cv"],
        "up_row_norm_cv": un["cv"],
        "feature_dim": int(feats.shape[1]),
    }


# k-means over 17,408 points in 10,240 dimensions is O(iters * n * E * d);
# keep E modest for the clustered plan so the experiment stays tractable.
PLANS = {
    "contiguous": (lambda ffn, E: contiguous_plan(ffn.intermediate, E), (8, 32, 128)),
    "interleaved": (lambda ffn, E: interleaved_plan(ffn.intermediate, E), (8, 32, 128)),
    "random": (lambda ffn, E: random_plan(ffn.intermediate, E, seed=0), (8, 32, 128)),
    "clustered": (lambda ffn, E: clustered_plan(ffn.gate, ffn.up, E, seed=0), (8, 32)),
}


def run(args) -> dict:
    out: dict = {"repo": REPO, "layer": LAYER,
                 "note": "real Qwen3.8-27B FFN weights, synthetic inputs"}
    ffn = load_real_ffn(LAYER)
    out["geometry"] = {"hidden": ffn.hidden, "intermediate": ffn.intermediate,
                       "params": ffn.params()}
    print(f"real FFN: hidden={ffn.hidden} intermediate={ffn.intermediate} "
          f"params={ffn.params():,}")
    out["weight_structure"] = weight_structure(ffn)
    print("weight structure:", json.dumps(out["weight_structure"], indent=2))

    # ---------------- exactness ----------------
    print("\n== exactness (all experts active vs dense, real weights) ==")
    exactness = {}
    for kind in ("gaussian", "student_t"):
        x = make_inputs(ffn.hidden, 16, kind)
        row = {}
        for pname, (fn, es) in PLANS.items():
            for E in es:
                p = PartitionedFFN(ffn, fn(ffn, E))
                err = p.dense_equivalent_error(x)
                row[f"{pname}_E{E}"] = err
                assert p.plan.covers_all(), f"{pname} E={E} plan does not cover all"
        exactness[kind] = row
        worst = max(row.values())
        print(f"  {kind:10s} worst relative L2 error = {worst:.3e}")
        assert worst < 1e-5, f"exactness violated for {kind}: {worst}"
    out["exactness_worst_rel_error"] = exactness
    # Derived from PLANS, not hardcoded: `clustered` deliberately skips E=128
    # (k-means over 17,408 points is the expensive plan), so a fixed string here
    # drifts out of step with what actually ran.
    checked = "; ".join(f"{p} E={list(es)}" for p, (_, es) in PLANS.items())
    n_combos = sum(len(es) for _, es in PLANS.values())
    print(f"  ALL EXACT within 1e-5  (checked: {n_combos} plan/experts combos "
          f"x 2 input dists -- {checked})")

    # ---------------- structure / oracle ----------------
    print("\n== contribution structure (does a top-k router have anything to find?) ==")
    oracle = {}
    for kind in ("gaussian", "student_t"):
        x = make_inputs(ffn.hidden, 256, kind)
        per_kind = {}
        for pname, (fn, es) in PLANS.items():
            for E in [e for e in es if e >= 32]:
                p = PartitionedFFN(ffn, fn(ffn, E))
                outs = p.expert_outputs(x)
                conc = contribution_concentration(outs)
                res = {k: oracle_topk_residual(outs, k)["mean_rel_residual"]
                       for k in (1, 2, 4, 8, 16) if k <= E}
                per_kind[f"{pname}_E{E}"] = {"concentration": conc,
                                             "oracle_mean_rel_residual": res}
        oracle[kind] = per_kind
        print(f"  -- {kind} --")
        for key, v in per_kind.items():
            best = min(v["oracle_mean_rel_residual"].items(),
                       key=lambda kv: kv[1])
            print(f"    {key:20s} participation_ratio="
                  f"{v['concentration']['participation_ratio']:7.2f}  "
                  f"best oracle residual (k={best[0]}) = {best[1]:.4f}")
    out["oracle_structure"] = oracle

    # ---------------- throughput ----------------
    print("\n== throughput (real weights, GPU if available) ==")
    tp = {}
    if torch.cuda.is_available():
        dev = "cuda"
        try:
            g = ffn.gate.to(dev).to(torch.bfloat16)
            u = ffn.up.to(dev).to(torch.bfloat16)
            d = ffn.down.to(dev).to(torch.bfloat16)
            gffn = DenseFFN(gate=g, up=u, down=d)
            x = torch.randn(1, gffn.hidden, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=dev)

            def timeit(fn, iters=30):
                for _ in range(5):
                    fn()
                torch.cuda.synchronize()
                ts = []
                for _ in range(iters):
                    t0 = time.perf_counter()
                    fn()
                    torch.cuda.synchronize()
                    ts.append(time.perf_counter() - t0)
                ts.sort()
                return ts[len(ts) // 2]

            t_dense = timeit(lambda: gffn.forward(x))
            tp["dense_ms"] = t_dense * 1e3
            for E, k in ((32, 2), (128, 4)):
                p = PartitionedFFN(gffn, contiguous_plan(gffn.intermediate, E))
                t_all = timeit(lambda: p.all_active_forward(x))
                idx = torch.topk(torch.rand(1, E, device=dev), k, dim=1).indices
                t_topk = timeit(lambda: p.topk_forward(x, idx))
                tp[f"E{E}_all_active_ms"] = t_all * 1e3
                tp[f"E{E}_topk{k}_ms"] = t_topk * 1e3
                tp[f"E{E}_all_active_overhead_vs_dense"] = t_all / t_dense
            print(json.dumps(tp, indent=2))
            del g, u, d, gffn
            torch.cuda.empty_cache()
        except torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError as e:
            tp["error"] = f"cuda OOM: {e}"
            print("  skipped: ", tp["error"])
    else:
        tp["error"] = "no cuda"
    out["throughput"] = tp

    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(args.out) or ".", exist_ok=True)
    with open(args.out, "w") as fh:
        json.dump(out, fh, indent=2)
    print(f"\nwrote {args.out}")
    return out


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="results/real_ffn.json")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    run(args)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
